# RectoPDF: priorities and delivery plan

Reviewed 2026-11-17 against `cdf7c1cc` (0.1.2 published; implementation at `ae0bf8a2`). Behaviour is in `SPEC.md`; product decisions are in `docs/OWNER-RULES.md`. The independent product, safety and architecture review is in [`reports/rectopdf-analysis-2026-11-17.md`](reports/rectopdf-analysis-2026-11-17.md).

## Where we are

RectoPDF is a shipped Mac reader with Save, page operations, highlights and side-by-side comparison. It is not yet the complete editor or agent workspace described by the later phases.

| Phase | Current status |
|---|---|
| 0–1: foundations, CLI and renderer | Shipped |
| 2–3: native reader | Shipped; reader correctness, responsiveness and assurance follow-ups remain below |
| 4: saving and editing | Save stages 1 and 2, core page commands and highlights shipped; remaining editing tools are future work |
| 5: tools | Side-by-side comparison shipped; redaction and Remove Hidden Data are the next agreed tools |
| 6: agent sidebar | Not built; follows the locator and safe-removal work |
| 7: release | 0.1.0, 0.1.1 and 0.1.2 published; remaining assurance is tracked below, not described as an unmade first release |
| R: restructuring | Closed by the owner 2026-11-15; real defects and regression guards remain, numeric restructuring targets do not reopen |
| 8: locators and libraries | Page identity and the first two matcher slices shipped; locator, retrieval and libraries remain |

## Next work, in order

This is the current priority order. The phase sections below retain the detailed issues and their evidence; they are not a second priority queue. Historical observations without a current reproduction remain investigation leads, not newly confirmed defects.

1. **CI budget follow-through.** Initial attribution is complete (`reports/ci-review-2026-11-18.md`). The baseline five green jobs at the analysis were 1,234/1,309/1,176/1,254/1,133 seconds: median 20:34 against 21:00, just 26 seconds of margin (read 2026-11-17). Delivery CI `44211481076` is green: longest job18:01, latest five-run median19:36 (84s headroom), budget unchanged. Its source-backed stage follow-through is in that report; this single run does not establish a causal speedup. Keep watching subsequent runs before choosing another optimization. The former ten-minute target stays retired.
2. **Remove known UI-thread waits and explain the remaining stalls.** Snapshot SVG is now prepared before paste (owner agreed, 2026-11-15; first slice below); its host callback takes prepared bytes without rendering. Use the short-stall record to find the actual work behind the measured ~40 ms passes against 16 ms. Keep sampling and `qa state` overhead separate from product work. Include the print-wait finding below; do not mark it flaky or solved by a larger timeout.
3. **Next feature milestone: 8.1 → 8.2, one open document.** The pure core types/codec/geometry are delivered; implement the resolver and link form next, reusing `recto-page/1`. Prove search → reference → resolve → retrieve, including after a file changes, with actor/protected-document policy unchanged. Blocks (8.3), libraries (8.5–8.7), graph service and the sidebar follow their prerequisites.
4. **Alongside the locator: design, then build redaction and Remove Hidden Data.** Owner's agreed sequence, 2026-11-15. D0's reviewed retention/crash contract is delivered (`design/removal.md`); next is its bounded pure retention-plan slice. It covers backup, kept pages, history/journals, old session formats, update copies, Find corpus and active/queued rendering. A rewrite of the PDF alone cannot satisfy removal. One writer, signed bytes unchanged, and independent re-extraction checks remain mandatory.
5. **Reader trust and the outstanding performance work.** Prioritize real text omitted by copy before cosmetic header suppression. Keep the hidden-text cases, heavy-page pointer reads, cold-open and signature work, protected-file editing decision, highlight identity faults and page-matcher recovery issues visible. These can supply small independent fixes alongside the feature work; they are not a general refactor.

**Large-file dependency:** the fork request is already in `/Volumes/EksternalHome/Koding/Rust/lopdf-lazy-engine/inbox/unread/2026-11-16-from-rectopdf-performance-review.md`: lazy first-page lookup and signatures without reading the whole edited file, with the older large-object amplification issues. RectoPDF does not modify or build a fork change here. Work that does not depend on its answer continues.

**Before the next PDFium upgrade:** include the renderer version in the corpus identity so cached character positions cannot disagree with live text. Before headless persistent indexing/libraries, settle the standalone CLI's key access: normal-state CLI uses `NoKey` today. Review the explicit-state-folder disk-key exception separately; it does not decrypt the normal app's corpus.

## Current run: started 2026-11-17

Owner: "I agree, can you get started on the 4 items?" (`OWNER-RULES.md`). First reviewed increments targeted for **18 November**; these are the first slices, not a promise to finish the complete redaction or retrieval roadmap that day.

| Track | First deliverable | Compartment / branch | Validation |
|---|---|---|---|
| CI and responsiveness | CI stage attribution from the last five green runs, then SVG prepared before paste | Coordinator; `codex/svg-ready`, snapshot tool and its headless tests | Recorded before/after wait, resource bounds, targeted tests, independent review |
| Durable retrieval | Reconcile locator choices; implement the smallest approved 8.1 core slice, then 8.2 | `codex/locator-core`, `recto-core::locator` | Capped/versioned hostile-input tests, no second page identity; design reviewed before code |
| Safe removal | Retention inventory, crash-state contract and reviewed implementation slices | `codex/removal-design`, `docs/design/removal.md` first | Source-backed inventory, one writer, signed-original behavior, recovery/remnant test plan |
| Reader trust | Reproduce and fix one real repeated-content omission in Copy | `codex/copy-fidelity`, `recto-index::chrome` | Regression fails first; named real cases; conservative output and measured cost |

Work used warm isolated worktrees and the complete testing brief. No overlapping source ownership; shared plan/owner decisions are the coordinator's. The CI diagnosis precedes new feature implementation, while design and correctness investigation proceed. Each completed slice gets its own review and commit. The reviewed code slices are integrated on one staging branch for a single final full check of the combined result before the main merge; the remaining scope stays open. The copy branch's first full check exposed existing macOS integration failures, so it is recorded as failed and not repeated while those are diagnosed.

## Release follow-ups carried forward

These are the still-open parts of the old release summary that otherwise would be hidden among completed work. Measurements are from the cited release reports, not new measurements by this review.

- [ ] Performance: an edited 2 GiB file's signature look reads the whole file after an update or first edit (3.5–5.3 s of one core); establish whether Save waits. The fork request includes it.
- [ ] Performance: explain the update's first-frame change (344 ms against the earlier 158 ms); keep page-drawn time separate from fade/rest time. Check a waking tab's sharp frame and the journal step's growth (~62 bytes against 28). Source: `reports/performance-2026-11-16.md`. The map and shaded-page memory misses are closed: allocator fix `d9a802e7`, final measured map 323/346/336 MiB at fit/~2x/~3x, shaded page 96.3–96.8 MiB. Final worker-rest policy is >64 MiB after 3 s, 48–64 MiB after 12 s (`fef4bf31`, then `fe5a3aff`, merged by `aa74a6aa`); do not implement the superseded >48 MiB/3 s instruction.
- [ ] Image selection: keep-rule placement and six calls to `ended`; Escape while a click's image lookup is pending; figures folded with a scan beyond the picture-list cap; a >20,000-object page's margin-click cost. Preserve the policy that a scan is not selected by a click. See `engineering/images-and-snapshots.md` and 0.1.2's review history.
- [ ] Verify the real host interactions not proved by quiet runs: a real pinch, image selection/Escape and Capture's context menu/crosshair. Current notes state these limits; no synthetic input or manual task is assigned to the owner.
- [ ] Before the first record-format bump: prune abandoned `session.v<N>/` records/journals by an agreed retention rule. Verify bring-forward handling of newer journals rather than assuming the 0.1.2 same-format downgrade fix covers it.
- [ ] Privacy decisions beyond Find encryption: the record's paths/place/last Find query remain in Time Machine; kept pages and original backups retain document content under the current rules. Any change needs its own product decision. Audit the remaining inventory against `PRIVACY.md`; encryption and removal of sleep pictures are already shipped.
- [ ] Page/contents placement: investigate pixel rounding below 1x, mid-page heading jumps, side-by-side rows and the sidebar's no-height report; retain the current source cases from `build/restore-read-late`'s review. Notices can overlap page content in narrow margins. A clean tab's reading place still follows its number after an insertion before it; use page identity when extending matcher slice 3.
- [ ] Contents pane empty after restart on CI: not reproduced in ten local restarts; preserve evidence and investigate (the previous plan's date was 18 November). The zoom label missing in shots was diagnosed as its pending fade, not missing drawing; the remaining settle/timer issue is below.
- [ ] Real update assurance: signed draft/carry checks against 0.1.1 and 0.1.0 passed for published 0.1.2. Still distinct: an ordinary installed 0.1.1 taking the update automatically, background/Space/full-screen behavior, and dark wake/locked-screen installation. Do not repeat the completed signing-secrets task.

### Security, recovery and tools leads retained from release reviews

- [ ] Find-key recovery/assurance: a lasting key refused as `NotAKey` or `NotEntitled` falls back to memory while existing lasting corpora remain unreadable until eviction, with the cause only in QA (N6; `find_key::fallback_source`, `corpus::SealedElsewhere`). Decide recovery and user-visible wording. Evidence still owed for before-first-unlock `errSecInteractionNotAllowed` (-25308), renewed signing certificate, a restored/person's Mac and another same-team app claiming Keychain Sharing access. These are not closed by the successful preflight.
- [ ] Key-handling hardening leads from the encryption review: the entitlements checker restricts the main executable's three entitlements but does not fully enumerate other helpers' claims; separately probe whether validating the running indexer's pid/signature before handing it a per-corpus key is useful and sound. The latter is a proposal, not a demonstrated helper-swap exploit.
- [ ] Two-journal recovery review (`build/carry-two-journals`, `70667e62`): the went-back notice can hide a genuine unreadable notice about lost lines in the older list; fuzz `merge_plan` with arbitrary operations; review recognition of resumed merges by operation comparison rather than a marker. Retain the generic `.not-merged` notice/purge issues below separately.
- [ ] CI/tool guard gaps: a superseded push can leave changed tools outside the next push's comparison range; trait implementations on counted types are not included in the ratchet (the `Doing` workaround was removed); add the recorded conflict-marker guard. These are specific correctness tasks for the next tools change, not a renewed method-count target.
- [ ] Image-selection review: a Full agent enabling the snapshot tool clears the person's selected image under the tool's current rule; the capped PDFium click walk is not fuzzed in process; a quit during a very-large-file image lookup waits for its open. Keep these separate from the fixed private-worker-on-every-click fault.
- Accepted limit, not a newly reproduced bug: after a clean file's change is heard but before its old tiles disappear, input can target the new file's same page position; an agent reusing an earlier page number can do the same. The change is announced in `where`. Durable references must account for this boundary.
- Intentional export, not a failed encryption claim: `recto index --agent` produces a plaintext Markdown copy in the agent folder. The owner chose "Ship as is, stated" (2026-11-15); it is in the privacy inventory and must not be described as protected by encrypted `text.pack`.

## Decisions still requiring the owner

Decisions are kept here, not mixed with finished implementation. They do not block unrelated work.

- Password-protected documents: support encrypted save, allow an explicitly unprotected Save As, or stop offering edits that cannot be saved. Current code safely refuses Save and Save As, but permits edits.
- Whether Full `save --replace` over an existing signed file is consistent with the unchanged-signed-bytes rule. Preserve the current refusal boundaries pending a decision.
- Whether an Undo queued behind an edit that is then refused should itself be dropped; whether SVG exports carry annotations.
- The remaining dark-paper borderline cases (off-page photo/dark sidebar, photo beside chart, white text on a mid-tone page); opening page 1 on the half-page margin rather than the current 24 points.
- The policy for keys typed immediately after ⌘F but before Find is published; the original review records the ordering concern.
- distillPDF dependency cleanup requires permission for that repository each time. No fork or other-repository push is authorized by this cleanup.
- Locator design: reconcile the research report's remaining choices with decisions already made, rather than asking all 19 again. The ordering, shared page identity and own `.recto`/`.kgl` choice are settled. Library privacy, grants, exports and naming are decided with 8.5's design.
- Quit/record lock lifetime: retries already run once a second (`3304f1a23`). Decide an explicit early unlock only if measurement of large-session quit shows it needed; the old proposed faster retry is implemented. Starvation is judged by the current wall/CPU stall rule; do not repeat the old question as if that rule were absent.

## Backlog maintenance

Cleanup requested by the owner 2026-11-17. The previous summary and completed release log are preserved in git at `cdf7c1cc:docs/PLAN.md`; their unresolved unique items are carried above or in the detailed phases below. Duplicate summary lists and retired feature counts were removed. Superseded sleep-picture tasks are closed by the owner's no-page-images decision, not deferred. Side-by-side comparison and highlights are marked shipped, with their actual remaining work retained.

Keep safety, authority, memory, writer and recovery issues until evidence closes them. A successful release does not by itself prove the sandbox OS matrix, signed/ad-hoc test parity, visible 120 Hz drawing, real RTL text or a corrected 24-hour soak. Do not resurrect structure-only counts or add a broad rewrite. Sizes in the historical phase entries are old estimates, not new commitments.

## Rules for every phase

1. **The CLI comes first.** A feature starts as a `recto` command; the window sends the same command to the same executor, with its actor. A feature without a command isn't finished.
2. **Done means** an agent can do it, see it and prove it: core method, CLI command, tests that name a failure, plus screenshots in light and dark once the app exists.
3. **Budgets are tests.** Memory and speed get measured on release builds with many large PDFs, and a miss is a bug.
4. **Follow explicit feature dependencies and ship incrementally.** Locator and safe-removal work may proceed alongside one another as the owner decided; each release satisfies its own gates. The original one-release scope has been fulfilled, not extended to every later phase.

## Phase 1: CLI and renderer (done)

The goal: an agent can do everything headless, as you'd expect from the finished product's agent interface. Later phases and the agent sidebar build on this.

**Core and commands**
- [x] One command API: versioned JSON, fixed exit codes, selectors that never guess
- [x] Tabs, tiers, sidebar model, tap rule: 1,000 tabs of a 330 MiB PDF add 1.4 MiB
- [x] Page-edit list with undo
- [x] Page-edit commands: move, rotate, delete, insert, undo, redo
- [x] Signature report command (placeholder: present, not checked)

**Renderer**
- [x] PDFium in a killable worker: memory limit with the 512 MiB, 1 GiB, lower-resolution sequence, per-page deadline, retirement at 64 MiB
- [x] `recto render`: any page to PNG, in strips under the same limits
- [x] `recto text`: normalised page text, with a hidden-text flag
- [x] `recto budget render`: idle worker, pages, peak, retirement, and a simulated viewport
- [x] Heavy image pages drawn in one pass per region, not one per tile: USGS map first view 2.1 s to 0.53 s

**The real agent interface**
- [x] `recto` as a client of a long-running session on a local socket (owner-only, no network)
- [x] Permission levels enforced for real: Off, Read-only, Control, Full, with the one command that asks for more
- [x] `recto wait` and `recto watch`, so agents never poll
- [x] Activity log of every agent action
- [x] Generated `SKILL.md` and `REFERENCE.md`, and `recto skill install`
- [x] Human-readable output by default, and `--json` on every command
- [x] **Blind agent test:** fresh agents with a one-line hint do real tasks and rate the CLI. Four rounds until every agent rated 8 or more; see the report below

**Done when:** fresh agents complete the tasks with no help beyond the skill, rate it 8 or more, and memory budgets hold with 200 tabs of your largest PDFs.

**Phase 1 report, 2026-11-07.**

| Agent's job | Round 1 | Round 2 | Round 3 | Round 4 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Reader, smallest model | 8 | 7 | 7 | **8** |
| Editor | 7 | 7 | 9 | **9** |
| Hostile and difficult files | 8 | 9 | 9 | **9** |
| Starts with no access | | 7 | 7 | **8** |

From round 3 the brief added that a clear refusal of access the agent wasn't given isn't a fault, so rounds 3 and 4 may read slightly higher. The agents can see this project's own rules, and reach `recto` through a small wrapper, so they aren't fully blind. No agent tried to get around the levels, and none obeyed text planted in a PDF. Answers were checked against `pdftotext` where they could be.

- **Found only by blind agents:** text in drawing order dropped `MUST` from its sentence, and an undo rule that trapped ordinary work.
- **Measured on release builds:** `recto status --json` about 3.2 ms typical and under 10 ms at worst, idle or with 1,000 tabs (budget 30 ms). Idle server 1 thread, about 1.7 MiB, 0 wake-ups in 30 s. 1,000 tabs of the 330 MiB PDF: about 3.0 MiB in total, handles unchanged. Whole-page export at 3× and 4× peaks at 465–469 MiB, above the 415–450 MiB accepted for heavy pages.
- **Not tested:** a peer from a real other user (simulated), a dead volume, a soak, Windows at runtime, a real right-to-left PDF. The real-terminal route ran only under a pseudo-terminal. Hot tabs at scale are Phase 2's budget tests.

## Phase 2: First real app

- [x] `recto-ffi` and the AppKit host, joined to the same session and socket as the CLI
- [x] **QA channel built with the first screen:** quiet runs, temp state, in-app `shot`, state reports, script runner
- [x] Hang watchdog: a 2 s stall fails the run
- [x] Window, sidebar, tap rule, contents with the rail
- [x] Page tiles on IOSurface, scrolling, pinch, blur to sharp. Scrolling and pinch are driven by the methods they call: no real gesture has run yet
- [x] Superseded, not deferred: no disk page previews. Owner 2026-11-15; `build/no-page-pictures` (`3aee9ec2`, guard `6e389ad5`). Sleep format 4 keeps facts only; pages fill in when drawn.
- [ ] Draw the next pages ahead while idle, within the memory limits
- [x] Dark mode with dark or white paper; focus view; agent icon as a placeholder
- [x] Contents in a scrolling area with a maximum height; other tabs and footer stay; virtualised, for 10,000 entries
- [x] Contents repositions and rests: dead zone, one eased move, no second move as a chapter closes. How it feels is for the owner to judge on a dev build
- [x] A shortened name shows in full on hover, for tabs and contents entries
- [x] Warm paper, a third paper colour, by a compositor tint with no re-render; `recto view --paper`
- [x] Budget tests: cold start, one document, scrolling, idle CPU, closed window (`recto qa budget`, off screen; numbers in `docs/ENGINEERING.md`)
- [ ] Spike on the MacBook: 120 Hz and on-screen memory
- [x] Sandbox spike: PDFium sandboxed in a nested helper, not XPC (`docs/research/2026-11-07-sandbox-spike.md`)
- [x] Build it: the worker's wall check and Mach drop, finding the helper in the bundle, the signed layout (`app/render-helper.sh`, `docs/ENGINEERING.md`, "Renderer")
- [x] The indexer and headings helpers read untrusted PDFs with lopdf, and the headings helper with distillPDF's image decoders too, outside any sandbox. The app parses the same files with the same lopdf in its own process, so sandboxing them alone wouldn't narrow lopdf's exposure; distillPDF's decoders are what only they add. Not the renderer's pattern: they open the file by path, the indexer writes its index, and both start render workers, which a sandboxed parent can't start in their own sandbox. Decide whether the headings helper gets a wall of its own. Owner, 2026-11-08: "Turn its image decoding off", so no wall. distillPDF has no switch for it, and needs none: the page-at-a-time path, the only one the helper calls, places an image from its dictionary and never decodes its samples. `tests/images.rs` (`the_headings_helper_never_decodes_an_image`) holds it with a 100 KB image that inflates to 108 MB: the helper peaked at 3 MiB on it, and distillPDF's own extraction of the same file took 207 MiB. The decoders are still linked into the helper; taking them out of the build needs distillPDF to make `image`, `jpeg-decoder` and `fax` optional, a change in distillPDF

**Done when:** you can read in it, an agent can screenshot every state without touching your screen, and the budgets are measured.

**You'll need:** to open a dev build; the MacBook for the 120 Hz check, run by an agent there.

## Phase 3: Complete reader

- [x] Native text selection: drag, double and triple click, select across pages, ⌘C, the system's highlight colour and I-beam cursor; `recto select`, `recto copy`, Services and a context menu from AppKit. Real mouse events, the Services menu itself and the compositor's blend aren't checked on a screen: a dev build for the owner is what shows them
- [x] Search corpus and index, with the text normaliser: `recto index`, `recto wait indexing-done`, and `recto search` answering from it, with `route` saying how
- [ ] Selecting, from its review, and the owner's to tighten in the planned hidden-text work: hidden text the check doesn't flag still reaches a copy, as pairs of white letters split by a visible dot, under `MIN_FLAGGED_RUN`, and white text printed over black, which has ink under it
- [x] Selecting, from its review: a pointer move at rest starts a worker to read its page, 0 to 4.5 MiB on a tiny file. Measured 2026-11-11 on heavy pages, release build, quiet run, under load 9 to 13, with the worker sampled every 100 ms: on the USGS map's p1 the read takes the worker to 185 MiB, twice the same, beside the 364 MiB its drawing takes, and the tree back to 24 MiB once it goes; NASA `ntrs20240002628` p51, 9 MiB. The UI thread's step for the move was 32 to 81 µs, and nothing waited. Within the map's named exception in SPEC §2 and rule 3, so nothing changed
- [ ] Selecting: a pointer resting on a heavy page reads its text for the I-beam, 185 MiB for a few seconds on the USGS map. A page already known to be heavy could wait for a press before it's read, with the I-beam over all of it meanwhile
- [x] Selecting, from its review: `setString` of the largest copy is synchronous on the UI thread. Measured 2026-11-11, release build, quiet run, under load 14 to 16, Select All and Copy of a made 1,500-page file of 17.9 M characters, which a copy stops at 16 MiB: 5 runs held the UI thread 11.9 to 15.7 ms each, 9.0 to 11.5 ms of it bringing the bytes over from Rust and 2.1 to 3.6 ms handing them to the clipboard, and the worst stall while copying was 22 ms. Rule 3's 2 s is met many times over and SPEC §2 has no budget for a copy, so it stays synchronous. `qa copied` says it in `write`. A quiet run's clipboard is one of its own, so the system's general clipboard wasn't the one measured: both go through the same pasteboard server
- [ ] Chrome in a copy, from the review of `build/render-fixes`, real content still left out: a table's header set off from its rows at the top of every page (`eu_dma` p65 to 66, a made `table.pdf`), a continued table's caption (NASA `ntrs20210023723` p42, 44), a box's heading at the same place on two pages (UN SDG 2023 p55 to 56), a map legend's label (NASA `ntrs20240002628` p271 to 272) and the labels at the top of an IRS form's identical copies (`f1099msc`). Caught since by the step and figures rules: a slide's axis labels repeated at the foot, a lone year at the foot of a one-page letter, axis labels on figure pages, footnote marks
- [x] Chrome in a copy, from the same review: numbered headings like "Article 12" and "Article 13" at the top of consecutive pages, which masking digits made the same line (a made `articles.pdf`). A row or a piece whose numbers differ from its twin's is chrome only if they step with the pages on two pages around, never fewer for fewer pages; one is enough only where the row holds the page's own number as a page's is set, a page-number piece or a number framed by a separator or a word for page (`L 119/2`, `XX:2`, `Page 5`, `5 | Report`). Its review (round 1) tightened it to that: long headings, headings carrying their page's number and two-page files had gone. Tests: `numbered_headings_at_the_top_of_pages_stay_in_a_copy`, `a_rows_numbers_step_with_the_pages_or_it_is_not_repeated`, `a_long_heading_steps_by_the_same_rule_as_a_row`, `a_heading_with_its_pages_number_stays`, `fewer_pages_around_never_lowers_the_bar`. `chrome_corpus` against main: part B unchanged but for p197's number (324 of 324), bench100 keeps 5 more lines, 2 captions and 3 of chrome (NASA `ntrs20240002628` p145 to 146's numbered footer, arXiv `quant-ph_2607.28598`'s last running head). Headings at the top of three pages in a row whose numbers count with them, as one-page articles' would, still go
- [ ] Chrome, the safe way round, from the same review: running heads whose numbers move by other than the pages stay in a copy, as "Articles 12-14" then "Articles 14-15" on an EU text's pages, or a two-up footer's "12 | 13" then "14 | 15", and those whose number only one page around agrees with and isn't framed as a page's, as arXiv `quant-ph_2607.28598`'s last running head
- [x] Chrome: a page number glued to the last word of a page by a line-end hyphen stays in a copy, as "neces-197" on part B p197, the only numbered page of 324 there that keeps its number. PDFium put its hyphen marker where the line's break was and the number straight after, below the line, so the two read as one line. Now whatever follows a hyphen well off its line starts a line of its own, either way along it (`a_page_number_after_a_line_end_hyphen_is_left_out`). `chrome_corpus` before and after: part B leaves out 324 page numbers of 324, against 323, and bench100's 12,381 lines left out are the same lines
- [ ] Chrome: a header or footer whose piece is short and whose row differs between pages stays ("July 2023" on RFC 9420's pages, "Preprint" beside a page number on some arXiv pages)
- [ ] Dark paper: a choropleth map of a few flat classes on white flips with the chart rule, as its fills are as flat as a stacked bar chart's and pixels alone don't tell them apart. None in bench100. SPEC says a map keeps its colours, so this needs another signal, such as the image's own name or many touching regions
- [ ] Dark paper: the first chart rule, white over 0.45 with at most 0.20 colour, predates the shading check and still flips a shaded object that's under a fifth of its image, as a small product shot on white. The shading check could apply to it too, after a look at the 84 earlier flips that span 4 lightnesses
- [ ] Chrome: about 20 real page numbers that start a row whose title changes from page to page stay in a copy (USGS running heads on 11 pages, NASA `ntrs20180005693`, `ntrs20210011071`, `ntrs20220005969` on 8, arXiv on 4), as the rule leaves a number that starts its row so that questions', headings' and footnotes' numbers stay. A number at the outer edge, mirrored on facing pages, could count, after a look at what else that takes
- [ ] Chrome: a page whose every line repeats on its neighbours, as a worksheet of identical questions, is all chrome, so a copy of it falls back to everything, its page number too
- [ ] Chrome, `recto text`: `chrome_spans` compares with the file's own pages either side, not the tab's positions, so after unsaved moves or inserts its neighbours are the file's, and a page from an added file compares with that file's
- [ ] Dark paper: an object shaded moderately, through only 3 lightnesses of one colour on white, still flips with the chart rule. The made balls span 4; a flatter rendering would pass

Review 1: the record from before is kept after that and taken if the version before can't read what was left (`coming_back_to_a_record_it_cant_read_takes_the_one_from_before`); Quit Anyway and SIGTERM aren't early ends (`a_quit_isnt_an_early_end_and_a_kill_is`); the misread counts tabs in the record on both sides; copies under the old names are moved (`copies_under_the_old_names_are_moved_to_the_new`); `release-zip.sh` checks the app's library is a release and ships no `test-*` file

- [ ] Installing during dark wake or a locked screen: not seen. By reading: the installer's waits are by `Instant`, which stops while the Mac sleeps, so a sleep mid-install only pauses it, and the new app's first frame and its own page need no screen. Look at it in the signed end-to-end run
- [x] A check by hand with the window closed says its result only in Settings, as the notice has nowhere to show: what's chosen from the menu leaves its answer for an app-modal alert in Rust's words when there's no window (`a_check_from_the_menu_with_no_window_says_so_in_an_alert`, `what_the_menu_starts_leaves_its_answer_for_an_alert`). An agent's `recto update check` has its answer in the command's and puts up nothing
- [x] Across an update, a minimised window or a hidden app comes back shown: both are in the record and `qa state` (`app.window.minimised`, `app.hidden`), stay so across an update and only then, with `qa minimise` and `qa hide` (`a_minimised_window_and_a_hidden_app_stay_so_across_an_update`), and coming back from an update that didn't work or wasn't installed (coordinator decision, 2026-11-11), with a late restore keeping what the host did (`a_restore_after_the_window_is_up_keeps_what_the_host_did`). What AppKit does with `miniaturize` and `hide` at launch hasn't been seen on a real desktop
- [x] Main red on CI's `check` job a third time (run 43040531427), four app tests. **Not the late path:** the report in the generated-contents failure says its first frame came at 329 ms, and all 191 app and restore tests pass here with the state read 1.5 s late. What CI's slower machine showed instead (`build/late-path`): a quiet run's window is never on screen, so AppKit lays it out only when something asks, and a report or `qa wait-settled` could come first, with a table of no rows made (`quitting_twice_in_a_row_changes_nothing`: the contents' accessibility rows empty after a restart), so the QA channel lays out its windows before either; the watchdog looked each quarter second and could miss the 2 to 2.3 s window of a stall on a starved machine, so a stall's end counts it too (`a_stall_the_watcher_didnt_see_is_counted_as_it_ends`); the quiet-reopen test wanted five reports in two seconds, which took the whole two there, so it waits for them; and five of six generated headings, likeliest a page whose hidden-text check missed its 10 s deadline at background priority (the test took 24 to 39 s here with every core busy), so such a page gets one more go with a fresh worker, and the test now says what was left out and why. A `late-state` stage in `tools/check.sh --full` runs 13 app and restore tests with the state read 1.5 s late and the window remade 300 ms after, 27 s here
- [x] Main red on CI's `check` job a fourth time (run 43054237265), on code identical to a green run, two test bugs, neither a crash (`build/ci-deaths`). **Generated contents, `session_ended` at 120.4 s:** the quiet run had its usual two minutes and the test would wait ten for a helper at background priority, so a slow generating ran into the run's own time limit; shown with the helper slowed to 22 s a page (same line, same answer, at 120.17 s), and passing in 135 s with the run outlasting the job's own limit. **An update to a release that ends after its first frame, `not_running` for 90 s:** the test caught the new version by polling for it answering, which it does for well under a second (on CI, about 0.4 s from opened to its end), and once missed nothing ran until the test started one; shown with the poll at 2 s, and passing with the same slow poll now that it waits on the installer's log and the processes at the app's path (`coming_back_to_a_record_it_cant_read_takes_the_one_from_before`, `a_new_version_that_keeps_ending_goes_back_by_itself`). **And CI says what it saw:** a test that finds the app gone prints its last messages, the update log and any crash report for it, and a failing check uploads the quiet runs' logs and the crash reports
- [ ] The headings helper's hidden-text check has a 10 s wall-clock deadline a page, at background priority, which CI's busy machine misses (run 43040531427), and each miss now costs a second try: a starved helper is slow, not stuck. A deadline in the worker's CPU time, or one that counts only while the worker was runnable, would tell the two apart. Proposal only, by reading; how long generating took on CI isn't known yet (the next failure's uploaded logs will say)
- [x] A quiet run once reported `app.activation` `accessory` instead of `prohibited` (`an_installer_killed_after_the_swap_loses_nothing`, in the report taken before the update, in `build/ci-deaths`'s full check, 2026-11-11). Something outside the app changes the policy, and `QuietRunGuard` heard of it only through the running app's KVO, a turn of the run loop or more later, so a report in between said so. Seen here too: `open -g` of a copy with the same identifier while a quiet run was up, `regular` in 2 of about 145 reports; registering another copy with `lsregister -f` showed nothing in 40. The guard now puts it back before every report and settle look, and says once in the run's messages that it had to (`a_quiet_run_is_prohibited_whenever_its_looked_at`, with the run turning itself `accessory` every 15 ms through `RECTO_QA_POLICY_NUDGE_MS`: 5 of 200 reports said `accessory` before the change, none after). What outside the app sets `accessory` isn't known
- [x] Main red on CI's `check` job again (run 43033806967): `find_in_the_sidebar_lists_steps_and_keeps_to_its_room`, "⌘F gives the field the keyboard". Commands are taken once the state's read, and the window's made again from it a moment later, so ⌘F went to the window about to go, and the new one came up with the page focused. A window that's up without the state now takes no input, and QA verbs that act on it wait, until it's made from the state, or 10 s (`what_is_done_before_a_late_state_comes_in_isnt_lost`, which widens the moment with `RECTO_QA_REMAKE_DELAY_MS` and fails without the change; `build/late-restore-2`). The app, restore and levels tests run with the state read 1.5 s late found two more: a selection and Find's hit went with the window made again (`a_selection_comes_back_when_the_state_is_read_late`)
- [x] Main red on CI's `check` job (run 43027452680): `everything_comes_back_after_a_quit` came back with the contents at no height. The state was read after the window's second of patience, and a late read didn't make the window again from it, so with the disk slow on purpose the size, full screen, appearance, Settings, every pane's place and the contents' scroll were all lost too, and just past the patience the first window wrote its default size over the state. Fixed by making the window again from the state, keeping the host's out-of-sight choices, and `qa wait-settled` waiting for it (`everything_comes_back_when_the_state_is_read_late`, `build/late-restore`)
- [x] Update tests failing under load (2026-11-11, `build/crash-loop-flake`). **A launch an update opened went up without its state:** with the state read after the window's second of patience, a closed window opened and one in the Dock or a hidden app came back shown (`a_closed_window_stays_closed_across_an_update`, `a_minimised_window_and_a_hidden_app_stay_so_across_an_update` in a full check at load 20 to 28). Such a launch now waits for its state, up to 20 s (`updates::restore_patience`, `Updater::may_be_reopened_by_update`), reproduced on purpose with a disk 2.5 s slow (`a_slow_disk_doesnt_bring_back_what_was_out_of_sight`, which fails without the change). **The crash-loop test counted launches that never happened:** a run whose process had gone still had its socket taking connections while it closed, so the next `qa start` was refused as already running, which the test counted as a run that ended early (`a_new_version_that_keeps_ending_goes_back_by_itself`, 2 of 13 group runs on `build/dev-updates`, and 2 of 6 here under load). It waits for the socket as `Run::wait_gone` does, and a refused start isn't a launch. Under the same load, two other whole-state comparisons differed once each by a moment's timing, not by state: `an_update_in_the_middle_of_things_changes_nothing` (the window's layout before it had settled) and `an_installer_killed_after_the_swap_loses_nothing` (the chrome still fading); left as follow-ups below
- [ ] Under load, a whole-state comparison across an update can catch the window mid-layout or the chrome mid-fade (`an_update_in_the_middle_of_things_changes_nothing`: contents and page sizes; `an_installer_killed_after_the_swap_loses_nothing`: `app.animations` `chrome_fading`), once each in 6 group runs with the disk loaded on purpose; logs kept in the crash-loop-flake report
- [ ] The control socket's lock has no patience at a launch, unlike the record's 600 ms: a new version opened while the old one's process is gone but still closing its files would start without its socket (a quiet run refuses). Not seen, by reading only

## Phase 4: Saving and editing

- [x] **Save stage 2, in the first release (owner: "We don't park anything")**, on `build/save-rewrite`: deletes by the streaming rewrite, signed files' unsigned copies, usage rights removed in place, kept pages only for undo past a delete, IRS form fields on added pages fillable, a changed added PDF not drawn, the rewrite fuzzed, measured, app QA and shots. Details in the items below

Moved here from phase 3 on 2026-11-08, as they belong to the rewrite (Save stage 2):
- Redaction save/retention requirements are tracked once under Phase 5 below; sleep files hold facts, not pictures, since 0.1.1.
- [x] On Save's gate: `save_prepared`, then `saved`, on the real path, and `never_in_pdf.rs` on that path rather than its stand-in writer; the reader takes up the new file; only the pages the history can put back are kept, so a PDF of any size can undo past a delete within 64 MiB of kept pages

- [x] On Save's gate, for the rewrite: check every page against the real file when writing (the save reads the file's own page count and every page asked for before writing, and `check_pages` reads the result back), as `validate_edits` checks against what a journal says of itself, which can be forged; and check the save marker again once it's on disk

- [x] Fork: append writer, reopen after append, revision chain (`work/append-writer` at `0e89b89` on the fork; RectoPDF still to repin)
- [x] Fork: streaming rewrite to a new file, copying only what's still used and without loading the whole PDF (`rewrite_atomically` at `598e61f`, used by Save stage 2), for deleting pages, replaced text, redaction, compress and hidden-data removal. SPEC section 6 needs it, and the only rewrite in lopdf today loads everything into memory
- [x] Agreement gate between lopdf and PDFium: page count, each page's shown size in order and whether either rebuilt the cross-reference data, once per file version off the UI thread, for the file and every added PDF; Save and Save As off with the reason in words (`recto_core::agreement`, `recto_agent::agreement`, ENGINEERING "The agreement gate")
  - [ ] Follow-up: the window's Save icon looks only at the tab's own file; a disagreeing PDF pages were added from is refused at the save, with an alert, not greyed beforehand
  - [ ] Follow-up: annotation counts and signature byte ranges (research §6.3) aren't compared, as PDFium needs each page loaded for them; worth it once signing or annotations ship
  - [ ] Follow-ups from review round 2: a look's deadline isn't checked around `File::open` or the worker's start, only between pages; saves' own looks aren't capped (each is a person's or an agent's save, one per tab); a save has no one overall deadline across its files' looks; Save As isn't greyed for an unedited file whose readers disagree (the window looks only once there are edits; the save refuses it); a renderer-only tab's links are still read through lopdf; a protected file past lopdf's limits doesn't fall back to PDFium, as the password's needed for both
  - Accepted limit (review round 2): a version is told apart by its `FileStamp` (size, modification time to the nanosecond the file system keeps, device, inode), so a file rewritten to the same size and time on the same inode reads as the same version
  - Accepted limits of the gate (review round 1): sizes stand for pages, so these pass unseen: two pages of the same size swapped; a page whose `/Rotate` is 0 to one reader and 180 to the other; pages of the same size with different content; any page past 10,000 that isn't among those sampled; and a different choice of object for the same number when neither reader rebuilt the index. A reduction of the risk, not a proof (research §6.3)
- [x] Native Save, Save As, close prompt, atomic writes, one backup (done: stage 1 by 745a12e1, stage 2 by 575d4415; the known limit below stays)
  - [x] Stage 1, by append: turns, moves and pages added from other PDFs. `recto_core::save`, `recto save`, `recto save-as --out [--replace]`, ⌘S, the Save icon beside the window's controls, File > Save As… on the system's sheet, File > Close (⌘W) asking Save, Don't Save or Cancel, a failed save's sheet, the red button's dot, one backup of the original on the first save (`backups.noindex`, 30 days within 1 GiB), attributes and ACL carried on Save and quarantine on Save As, hard links, read-only files and folders and a file changed meanwhile refused whole. QA: `qa button save`, `qa sheet-answer`, `qa save-as`, `qa state`'s `save_button` and `sheet`
  - [x] Stage 2, by the rewrite: deletes (`recto_core::save`'s plan goes to either writer through a `Sink`), a signed file's unsigned copy (decision 27: Save off with the icon saying why, Save As's sheet saying "The new copy won't be signed. The original keeps its signature.", the copy with every signature removed), usage rights removed in place (`RewritePolicy::remove_usage_rights`, at `598e61f`), and keeping only the removed pages for undo. QA: `qa state`'s `save_button.enabled` and `tooltip`, `copy_notice`; `qa button save` says why when Save is off. Tests: `recto-core`'s save tests, `a_delete_is_saved_by_rewriting`, `a_signed_pdfs_delete_goes_to_an_unsigned_copy`, and in the app `a_delete_saves_by_rewriting_and_undo_goes_past_it`, `a_signed_files_delete_turns_save_off_and_save_as_is_unsigned`, `usage_rights_alone_save_in_place_with_a_quiet_note`. Shots: `app/qa/save-shots.sh`

Release build, `crates/recto-core/examples/save_delete.rs`. The map's rewrite reports 240 objects missing: the file is damaged (qpdf rebuilds its cross-reference data and still doesn't find object 198), and MuPDF draws the original and the rewrite identically at 8 and 36 dpi
  - [x] Stage 2 fuzzed: `save_copy` also runs `rewrite_in_memory` (a delete in a hostile file, with and without pages added, through the fork's own verify). 206,891 runs in 180 s, under load, no failure, 52 MB peak
  - [x] Stage 2's review round 2 follow-ups: Save As onto a signed file's own name offers Save As (`a_signature_refusal_offers_save_as`); the signature look ends however its thread does, one per file and at most 4 at once (`a_signature_look_ends_however_it_ends_and_one_file_doesnt_hold_the_rest`); a blank signature field on an added page comes along (`an_added_pages_blank_signature_field_comes_along`); a `/V` with a `/ByteRange` is a signature whatever its `/FT` (`a_value_with_a_byte_range_is_a_signature_whatever_the_field_says`); field names compared decoded (`field_names_are_compared_as_text`); the name look's 100,000 bound tested on its own (`a_form_past_the_field_bound_is_refused_by_the_name_look`); `qa save-from` refuses a file whose signatures can't be looked at (`save_from_refuses_a_file_it_cant_look_at`)

No real form in the corpus comes near it; raise it, or look lazily, if one does
- [x] Undoing a delete past a Save keeps only the removed pages, within 64 MiB per PDF, in place of a whole-file clone (owner, 2026-11-07): `recto_core::save::keep_pages` writes only the pages the history can put back, by the rewrite; `AddedSource::holds` maps the history's page numbers onto it
- [x] Save's gate, from the streaming rewrite's review, for the rewrite (the 1 MiB reader is done): a warning whenever `objects_missing` isn't empty, because MuPDF and poppler can repair what the copy drops; `document_data_kept` said honestly after a delete, in words; `remove_object` never called (`clippy.toml` refuses it)
- Remove Hidden Data's offer after a delete is part of Phase 5's removal feature below.
- [ ] Fork: a big parsed object costs about 365 times its source bytes in memory during a rewrite; find the copies
- [x] Save's gate: no edits, undo, redo, discard or close while a save writes (`Session::begin_save`, `nothing_changes_a_tab_while_it_is_saved`), and a hand-off waits for a save from the window

- [x] Fork: blank signature fields stay when signatures are removed (`1336343`, with a fix for an exponential walk in it, on the fork's `main` at `e5f4971`). RectoPDF is pinned to it, and `a_blank_signature_field_stays_through_a_rewrite` runs. The same commit makes `tests/rewrite.rs` build without the two fuzz-found PDFs that `*.pdf` in `.gitignore` keeps out of a checkout

- [ ] Form filling, with appearance generation

- [ ] Failure tests from the research: no lost edits on any exit path, never damage a signed file
  - [x] For the edits the first release ships (a turn, a move, pages added, a delete, every page rotated): `restore/exits.rs` puts one of each on a tab of its own and leaves by a quit, a kill, a hand-off, sleep and a kill while asleep, the window closing, printing and exporting, and closing each tab through its prompt (Cancel, Don't Save, and Save with an undone PDF's name and text only in the history), plus a kill in the middle of a real save. Every edit comes back with whose it was and its undo and redo, and no PDF changes by a byte; Save writes nothing of the history. Signed files: `a_signed_pdfs_bytes_never_change` and the stage 2 tests. Known and by design (decision 25): a kill keeps what the journal has, and edits reach it after 2 s of quiet or 5 s in a burst; after a crash in the first minute, tabs come back from their journals under new numbers, and one with nothing unsaved doesn't come back
  - [ ] The same for annotations, forms and text edits, once they ship
- [x] Close the gap in edit ownership: below Full an edit, undo or redo by an agent that would push the owner's steps out of the history is refused (`history_full`)
- [x] A file lopdf refuses for hitting a limit must not fall back to PDFium in `crates/recto-agent/src/loader.rs`: a hostile file with 100,000 `/Prev` sections takes PDFium 3.4 s to load, though the fork's cap refuses it in about 3 ms. Now `OpenError::TooComplex` for any of the fork's limits (revisions, entries, structure, object, stream, resolution depth, page count, page-tree depth). Coordinator's decision in review round 1: such a file still opens to be read through PDFium, renderer-only, so its edits are refused and Save is off (`a_file_refused_at_a_limit_opens_to_be_read_with_edits_refused`), and `TooComplex` is what's said when PDFium can't read it either. Each worker start then pays PDFium's own cost of reading it (3.4 s for the 100,000-section chain), which is accepted
- [x] Save uses only the fork's `append_atomically` with `prepare_replacement`, and `clippy.toml` refuses `Writer::write_object`, the eager `Document` and `IncrementalDocument` saves, `copy_revision_to` and `RewriteChanges::remove_object` by name; test fixtures that build encrypted files say why they're allowed
- [x] Opening a file with lopdf never runs on the UI thread: a 2 MiB hostile file relisting a million numbers takes 3.7 s of CPU and 96 MiB, bounded but over the 2 s stall rule. Done by `40be99e` (opening goes to a read thread); proved by `opening_a_slow_hostile_file_never_holds_the_window`: three such sections (`recto_testpdf::relisted_xref`) took 3.5 s to open by `recto open` and by File > Open Recent in the debug app, under load, while the window's worst wait was 15 ms
- [x] **Bug, found reshooting Save's screenshots (2026-11-08):** a save changes the view. After `page rotate 1 --by 90` and `save` on `astro-ph_GA_2607.28278.pdf`, the zoom shown goes from 128% to 97% and the pages shrink. A save must leave zoom, place, layout and selection as they were. (First reported as page 2 turning white on dark paper; the coordinator's look at the shots found page 2 dark before and after, and the brighter pixel sample came from the zoom change.) On `build/save-view`

The session now says which version a save moved a tab on from (`Session::saved_from`), the page map carries it (`PageMap::follows`), and the page view keeps its layout (`PageLayout::as_saved`), place, zoom and selection. Tests: `a_save_leaves_zoom_place_layout_and_selection_as_they_were`, `a_save_says_which_version_it_moved_on_from_and_only_while_it_holds`, and in the app `a_save_leaves_the_view_as_it_was`. What's drawn is still dropped and drawn again from the new file, as the red frames' rule has it

It found that a crash while a save writes lost the edits made in the 2 to 5 s before it, which hadn't reached the journal: a save now writes the journals first (`SaveSupport::before_writing`)

**Done when:** edited files open correctly in other readers and pass external validators, with no data-loss failures across the test matrix.

**Highest risk:** text editing and forms. If text editing stalls, you'll hear early, along with the options.

## Phase 5: Tools

- [ ] Compress: Low, Medium, High to a fixed DPI, with size estimates
- [ ] OCR: Tesseract in a helper, Make Searchable, invisible layer with a Unicode font
- [ ] Password protection
- [ ] Text/content-difference comparison of two PDFs, read-only (SPEC §6: "Text first, read-only"). Side-by-side comparison of two or three PDFs already ships (`recto compare`, `recto-session/src/compare.rs`, `engineering/comparison.md`); its synced-scroll/zoom follow-ups stay in Phase 3. Panes do not close this separate tool.
- [ ] Redaction and Remove Hidden Data, each with independent re-extraction proving the content is gone. Design the retention/crash contract before building: no recoverable removed content in original backups, kept undo pages, history/journals, old session-format folders, update copies, corpus, active/queued rendering or facts derived from removed text. A redacting save keeps nothing of that content, enforced by the writer/save hooks, including after a crash before or after rename and Save As with a record behind. Ordinary delete backups remain by the owner's existing decision. Offer Remove Hidden Data beside the honest note about document data a delete retained. Never rewrite signed existing bytes.
- [ ] Watermarks, Bates numbers
- [ ] Create form fields, measure tools

**Done when:** every tool works through the CLI and the UI, with its failure tests.

## Phase 6: Agent sidebar

After the locator and redaction/Remove Hidden Data, as the owner decided 2026-11-15. It uses the shared CLI and corpus; the decision does not require completion of every library step first.

- [ ] Spike: the locked-down `claude` command, using a small amount of your quota, with your approval
- [x] Per-document folder and `manifest.json`, made from the corpus on demand by `recto index --agent`
- [ ] First-prompt facts, and pointing the run at that folder; the agent requests what it needs
- [ ] Hidden-text and prompt-injection tests
- [ ] Panel: visible steps, clickable citations, token count
- [ ] Setup steps when `claude` isn't installed; first-use consent per document
- [ ] Protected documents: reading a password-protected document's text needs the user's consent for that document, per the agent report's §5.5. Until then `recto text` refuses with exit 8
- [ ] Room for a second CLI
- [ ] **Blind agent test, round 3**

**Done when:** answers cite the right pages on real documents, and a hostile PDF can't reach beyond the document's folder.

**You'll need to decide:** whether a public release waits for Anthropic's written answer on subscription use, and whether to add an API-key route.

## Phase 7: Release

**Structure quick fixes, before the release** (owner, 2026-11-08: "let's do the quick fixes now before release, then add the large restructuring into the plan before we start on the other features"). Steps 1 to 3 of `docs/reviews/2026-11-08-architecture.md` §4.6, at the first merge point with no branches open:
- [x] Step 1: drifted docs fixed, `Theme.sidebarWidth` deleted; every person-originated `CoreCall` decided for the update hand-off freeze (`CloseTab`, `ToggleSection`, `ContentsAction`, `CloseAnswered` held back; `CloseAfterSave`, `Unlocked` and the Finder's news let through, as ends of what's under way), with an exhaustive test; `menus::enable_rule` and `table::EVENTS` with no default, with tests that every menu id has a rule and every `Event::new` in the code is listed
- [x] Step 2: restore's `KNOWN_PATHS` and `EXCEPTIONS` sorted and split into per-area files under `tests/restore/known/` and `tests/restore/exceptions/`, with a test that each is sorted with no duplicates and in its own area; the app test harness (`Run`, `app_path`, `settled`, `wait_for`, document makers) moved into `tests/common/app.rs`
- [x] Step 3: `rustfmt` adopted (`max_width` 160; comments left as written, as `wrap_comments` is off by default and nightly-only) in one mechanical commit with no branches open, `.git-blame-ignore-revs`, and `cargo fmt --check` as `check.sh`'s first stage, quick and full. The fuzz workspace, built on nightly, isn't formatted or checked

- [ ] Budget (report of 2026-11-08): opening a 10,000-page file reads every page object first, `lopdf`'s `IndexedReader::page_map` walking the tree with a `pread` each, scattered over the file: 1.07 to 1.09 s for `recto local F facts` on a made 2 GiB file not in the cache, 0.08 s once it is, 907 of 930 samples in that walk. SPEC wants 600 ms cold for page 1 sharp. Each of 1,000 tabs of APFS clones pays it too (about 1.5 s a tab in `qa budget`), as clones don't share a cache. Read the page tree lazily, by `/Count`, as far as the page asked for (the fork, so the owner's go-ahead to push it)
- [x] Budget (report of 2026-11-08): one blank step on a page already drawn, in a `qa sweep 6` that starts from a settled page: `rfc9110`, `goto 64`, settle, then `qa sweep 6 --for 5s` six times read 1, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, and `qa budget`'s sweep read 1 on `rfc9110` and on the made 2 GiB file. The last report read 0. SPEC wants 0 blank frames on seen pages. Find which page and why it had nothing attached (`count_blanks` in `recto_reader::tiles`), with a test. **Found** (`build/budget-misses`, 2026-11-11): a page in view by less than a pixel. Each blank step, 2 in 21 sweeps on a release build with a probe in it, was page 104 with the view's top 0.004 points above its foot, inside the part of a pixel that `device_size`'s rounding leaves without a tile. `visible_cells` found no cell there, so refreshing took the page's preview and tiles off, and `count_blanks`, going by `pages_between`, called it bare though nothing of it could be seen. Not eviction (0 discards lost, the preview still held), a race, or the first sweep's direction. `count_blanks` now goes by the cells drawing goes by (`shows_a_pixel`). Release builds taking turns, under load 3.6 to 7.1: the base read 1 blank seen step in 16 sweeps, this 0 in 16, then 0 in 24 more. Test `a_sweep_over_seen_pages_has_no_blank_step`: 26 before, 0 after
- [ ] Budget (report of 2026-11-08): waking a sleeping tab, `rfc9110` p40 from its sleep file, went sharp in 90, 100, 100, 101 and 238 ms over 5 runs under load 4 to 5, against 100 on a text page. First frame 1.5 to 2.9 ms and no blank frame meet theirs. Find what the 238 ms run waited on. **Found, not fixed** (`build/budget-misses`, 2026-11-11): the woken tab's new worker's first tile, ours, not the machine's. 25 wakes on a release build with a probe timing each job, load 4 to 12: sharp 94 to 427 ms, p50 257, 22 over 100; the worker's start and open 12 to 62 ms, its first tile 70 to 331 ms, the 11 tiles after it 6 to 74 ms in all. Of that first tile the draw was 1.7 ms: the rest was the worker's first `IOSurfaceLookupFromMachPort`, setting up the IOSurface client after the Mach drop, 55 to 130 ms, with `libxpc` trying a right the drop had taken, about 450 assertion failures logged a worker and about 100 ms of processor. A walled worker drawing into shared memory has no such cost (3.5 ms first tile), nor does a bare one into a surface (5.5). It's every new worker's first tile, so a cold open's and a tab switch's too. 25 more wakes of the base taking turns with a fix, load 4.4 to 6.4: base p50 87.1 ms, p95 91.7. **A fix that works and can't go in:** setting the client up before the walls (one pixel's surface made and let go, about 12 ms there) made the first tile 3 to 9 ms and the wake p50 41.1, p95 50.4, the worker 7.1 MiB after its first tiles against 9.3, but the client's setup then reaches whatever registers the helper's bundle with Launch Services, so `no_copy_but_the_one_install_is_left_registered` failed in two full runs (the app's helper, and the sandbox tests' unsigned one, every run: 1 left with it, 0 without). A lookup of a null port does the same. Next: find which service the client asks (the same one that fails after the drop), and set the client up without it, or draw a new worker's first tiles into shared memory while a second thread sets the client up after the walls. The commit and its test (`a_new_sandboxed_workers_first_surface_tile_costs_what_a_shared_memory_one_does`, 64.4 ms into a surface against 11.6 into shared memory before) are kept off `main`, in the worker's report

One helper that makes a fresh folder, used by all Done (`build/hygiene`): `recto_testpdf::TempDir`, a folder made with `create_dir` and never one already there (the next count on `AlreadyExists`), private, and removed with what's in it on drop (`a_temp_dir_is_always_fresh_and_goes_with_its_files`). `TempPdf` and every test and fuzz target that named a temp folder by its process id use it, 58 places in 13 crates; recto-core's own `TempPdf`, one folder shared by every file of a test binary, is the shared one now. Left as they are: temp files for atomic writes, paths that must never exist, and `free_folder`'s names, which already pass over a taken one. The same reuse in shipping code is its own item below
- [x] A quiet run's report said `app.activation` `accessory` once more, read through the guard (`QuietRunGuard.policyAsLookedAt`, put back and read with nothing between): `update::an_update_in_the_middle_of_things_changes_nothing`, `accessory` then `prohibited` across the update, in `build/hygiene`'s first full check (2026-11-11, app-tests at `-j 4`, another worker's builds running). It passed alone straight after. If a read straight after `setActivationPolicy(.prohibited)` can still say `accessory`, the put-back didn't take: check its return value, and say in the run's messages when it fails. Done (`build/hygiene-2`): reproduced with three quiet runs side by side, each turned `accessory` every 15 ms and opened by `open -g` of its own clone every other look: 2 of 900 reports said `regular`. Neither the set's answer nor one read can be taken alone: a set from `accessory` said it took and the read straight after said `regular`, as Launch Services' change landed in it, and a set from `regular` said it hadn't while the read said `prohibited`. The guard now puts it back until a read says `prohibited`, at most 8 times in a row, then again on later turns of the run loop, one at a time and at most 50, says each case once in the run's messages, and a report says the guard's own last read. After: 1,800 of 1,800. A test switch, `RECTO_QA_POLICY_NUDGE_MS=<ms>,in-put-back` (test builds only, as the nudge is), makes each look find the policy changed and lands one more change inside its put-back: with one try, the old way, 200 of 200 reports said `accessory`; with the guard's tries, none do (`quiet::a_change_landing_in_the_put_back_is_put_back_too`)
- [x] `registrations::no_copy_but_the_one_install_is_left_registered` failed on CI (run 43101058944, `main` at `97ce800`, everything else green): `app/build.noindex/own-34946-0/RectoPDF.app`, the reopen test's own copy, was still registered. Done (`build/hygiene-2`): a copy opened and dropped straight away was left registered here too, as one `lsregister -u` before its run had quit, or before the system had finished registering the launch, was undone. `OwnCopy`'s drop now ends every run of the copy (TERM, then KILL, each bounded), forgets it and what's inside it, and looks at the system's dump, again until the dump has it gone twice in a row with no run left, within 20 s, then removes the folder; `recto_platform::registry::forget_until_gone` is the forgetting with a look. One queue with one thread, capped, latest per tab, as AGENTS rule 1 has it. A test that opens 40 tabs doesn't reach the pile; it needs the thousand. Done (`build/one-sleep-writer`): `recto-ffi/src/sleep_queue.rs`, one writer only while there's work, 8 tabs and 16 MiB of pictures at most, newest per tab, the oldest losing pictures first over the bytes and dropped and counted past 8 tabs, a waiting flush let go first. 77 tabs leaving with sleep writes 5 s late, release build under load: threads 19 to 88 before and 19 to 20 after, footprint 17.0 to 71.7 MiB before and 17.0 to 22.4 after (`tabs_leaving_in_bulk_with_slow_sleep_files_have_one_writer`, which fails on the old code; ENGINEERING, sleep files)
- [x] A quiet run's activation policy read `accessory` in one whole-state comparison across an update (`coming_back_to_a_record_it_cant_read_takes_the_one_from_before`, sleep files slow, seen once in three runs): the same as the one above, which `a72afc5` fixed (a quiet run is prohibited whenever it's looked at), found apart in `build/slow-mode`
- [x] `heavy-test.sh`'s "solo waits for all, and runs before later jobs" failed once (expected `n1 solo n2`, got `n1 n2 solo`) under two other workers' load, and passed on the next run (2026-11-11). Two more of its order checks failed the same way: its jobs waited for the real Mac's memory, which the fixed pauses didn't allow for. It runs with `MIN_FREE=0` now, as `check-test.sh` does (`build/slow-mode`)
- [x] **Ghost RectoPDF tiles in the Dock** (owner, 2026-11-12: "there is like a billion rectopdf instances"; `build/dock-ghosts`): the two quiet tests that `open -g` a copy of the app (`a_quiet_run_stays_quiet_when_the_system_opens_it_again`, which reopens a run every few looks) left tiles for processes long gone, until `killall Dock`; every full check added more. **Cause, measured by counting tiles in screenshots after a clean Dock:** Launch Services makes a quiet run it reopens `regular` for about a millisecond, and the Dock adds a tile that outlives the process and the deleted bundle. It's the Dock's, not the guard's: putting the policy back through `accessory` first (regular, accessory, prohibited) left 8 tiles from 4 runs, as the plain way did, so the guard isn't what keeps the tile. Six runs reopened 4 times left 12, 3 runs 3 times left 3, 6 runs twice or once left 0, but one open of a copy through the test's own start left 1, so no number of opens is safe for a regular copy. The two tests together left 6 tiles (the coordinator's run, about 20). A copy with `LSUIElement` is made `accessory` by Launch Services instead, and left 0 tiles from 6 runs reopened 4 to 8 times, and 0 from the same two tests after the fix. **Fix:** `OwnCopy` (the tests' own copy of the app) is always that copy: `LSUIElement`, and an ad hoc signature on the outside only, as `copy_app` in the restore tests does; every test opens it through `OwnCopy::open_quietly`. The reopen test now expects `accessory`, never `regular`. **Not covered:** nothing in a test makes the real, regular bundle regular any more (the put-back from `regular` is the same code as from `accessory`, `QuietRunGuard.keepProhibited`, and `qa nudge` covers the race), and the Dock can't be read without an accessibility permission, so `a_copy_the_system_opens_is_never_a_regular_app` asserts the copy's plist and signature, and the reopen test asserts the run was never reported `regular`. Other paths that open a bundle: the restore update tests' copies are already `LSUIElement` with their own bundle id; `recto-update`'s `open` and `tools/dev-build.sh`'s `open -g` reopen the real, regular install on purpose and only if it was running
- [x] **Known failing on CI, due 2026-11-12:** `a_search_in_the_app_gets_its_index` and `the_sidebar_offers_generated_contents_and_labels_them` skip when `CI` is set, as the indexer and the headings helper run at Darwin background priority, which GitHub's virtual Macs starve (3 runs on 2026-11-11). `build/helper-qos` moves them to utility; it removes both skips. Done (`build/helper-qos`): the session starts both helpers through `recto_platform::helper` with `posix_spawnattr_set_qos_class_np(QOS_CLASS_UTILITY)`, a clamp every thread of the helper and its render worker inherit (`ps -M`: 20, against 31 and Darwin background's 4), and the helpers no longer lower themselves; the search test waits up to three minutes in waits of a minute rather than once for 60 s; both skips are gone
- [x] **Known failing on CI, due 2026-11-12:** `quiet::a_quiet_run_is_prohibited_whenever_its_looked_at` skips when `CI` is set: on GitHub's runner 1 of 200 reports said `regular` (run 43085841110), a policy the test's nudge never sets, so something put a quiet run in the Dock's mode for a moment. Find what, fix it, remove the skip Done (`build/last-flakes`): **Launch Services** sets it. An `open` of a bundle that reaches a running quiet run makes that run regular before it sends the reopen, as a notification on the main run loop (`runningApplicationNotificationCallback` in the change's own stack; the reopen event some 40 ms after), and nothing in the app calls `setActivationPolicy(.regular)` in a quiet run. The guard put it back about a millisecond later, but a report read the policy at the end of building the rest, after the guard had looked at its start: 9 of 1,800 reports said `regular` with 240 reopens of the run's bundle, and 0 of 1,800 once a report reads it through the guard (`QuietRunGuard.policyAsLookedAt`, put back and read with nothing between). The only `open` of the build in the suite was the reopen test's, which reaches whichever run of that bundle Launch Services picks, another test's too: a quiet run reached by a shell `open` of the build said in its messages it had been made regular, and failed. That test now starts and opens a copy of its own (`OwnCopy`, an APFS clone beside the build, forgotten by Launch Services and removed when dropped), which an `open` of a clone reaches alone (5 of 5 reopens, whichever run started first), opens it every few looks, and checks each look and that it was reached. The guard says each policy it put back once, so a test's `accessory` doesn't hide the system's `regular`. The skip is gone; with the reopen test and three other app tests at `-j 4` under `RECTO_QA_SLOW=ci` (the coordinator's case), all passed. Not explained: once in 23 runs of the two quiet tests side by side, the prohibited test's run said it had been made regular, so something reached it; it no longer shows in a report
- [x] **Release tooling for 0.1.1** (`build/release-tooling`, 2026-11-15): the draft's notes from `docs/releases/X.Y.Z.md` (`tools/release/notes.sh`, refusing a missing file, another version, a `<!-- PLACEHOLDER: -->` line and a body over 100,000 characters; the release gate asks first), the version bump as one script with `--check` (`tools/release/bump.sh`), the draft's checks as one script with the previous release's own updater code as a verdict (`tools/release/check-draft.sh`, `verdict.sh`), and the state carried across versions and back in quiet runs (`tools/release/check-carry.sh`). `docs/RELEASING.md` has the sequence
- [x] **RectoPDF 0.1.1 is published** (2026-11-16 01:25 UTC, tag `v0.1.1` on 3d70504c, release run 43783471758: gate 834 s, Mac build 666 s, signing 68 s, draft 9 s). On the draft's own bytes before publishing: `check-draft.sh` every check passed (0.1.0's own updater takes it; Developer ID, notarized, Gatekeeper `Notarized Developer ID`; the Find key's entitlement on the main executable alone), `check-carry.sh` exit 0 with the known going-back faults below and no other. After: the public `latest.json` names 0.1.1 and the public zip's sha256 is the one in it (e5a58059…). The first tag (on 91d7bd3e) was withdrawn by the gate after an hour, a fault of the gate's and not of the build: fixed in 3d70504c, one independent review ("OK to tag with").
- [x] (done 2026-11-16, `build/release-0.1.2-tools`) **The release gate, from that review, before 0.1.2's tag:** count only CI runs on `main` and look past a cancelled newest run to an older green one on the same commit (`gh run list … --branch main --limit 20`, the first not cancelled); refuse a tag whose commit isn't on `main` (`git merge-base --is-ancestor HEAD origin/main`). Notes from it: a re-run that went green counts as a pass (say in RELEASING.md whether that's meant); a push that cancels an earlier push's run on `main` means the earlier push's changed tools were never tested by CI (a hole in CI's scope, `tools/check-scope.sh`, not the gate).
- [x] (fixed 2026-11-16, `build/carry-two-journals`) **To be fixed before 0.1.2 is published: a tab that went back to 0.1.0 and forward again says its edits couldn't be read back, though they all are.** Found by `tools/release/check-carry.sh` (run D, 2026-11-15): 0.1.0, on 0.1.1's state, leaves a version-2 journal alone and writes the record with `"notice":"edits_unreadable","journal":null` for that tab; 0.1.1 next time reads the journal whole (its highlight is listed) and restores the notice as it stands. Likely place: on restore, a record's notice that edits couldn't be read is checked against the journal as it reads now, not taken as it stands. Met by a copy that went back to 0.1.0 and later goes forward: by the updater itself (0.1.1 not saying `launch-ok`, or three early ends in its first week, `docs/RELEASING.md`), or by hand; it then holds 0.1.1 against the version and moves forward at 0.1.2, which is where it would meet this, so the fix belongs in 0.1.2 and not holding 0.1.1 (coordinator, 2026-11-15). Acknowledged as `fault:D.notice.edits_unreadable` in `tools/release/carry/0.1.0-to-0.1.1.txt`, which the check says as a known fault until the line goes
- [x] (fixed 2026-11-16, `build/carry-two-journals`) **To be fixed before 0.1.2 is published: an edit made in 0.1.0 on a tab with highlights, after going back, hides the highlights in 0.1.1 and calls it "open in two tabs".** Found by `tools/release/check-carry.sh` (runs E and F, 2026-11-15): 0.1.0 starts a journal of its own (`journal-<id>-3.log`, version 1) beside 0.1.1's version 2, which stays on disk byte for byte; 0.1.1 next time reads only 0.1.0's, shows the turn and no highlight (`highlights` lists none), and the tab says `edits_not_merged` ("This PDF was open in two tabs with different unsaved edits"), which it wasn't. Likely place: a tab with journals of two versions takes both, or says which it left and why, and never the two-tabs words. Met as the line above is, so fixed before 0.1.2 is published for the same reason. Nothing is lost from disk; acknowledged as `fault:F.highlight_hidden` and `fault:F.notice.edits_not_merged` in the same file
- [x] The `corpus` fuzz target leaves a `corpus-<pid>` folder in the temp folder for every process that runs it (fuzz audit, 2026-11-11): make one fresh folder per run and remove it at the end. Done (`build/last-flakes`): each check makes its own `corpus-<pid>-<n>`, never one already there, builds the corpus it damages in it, and removes it as it ends, by a panic too (`a_run_leaves_no_folder_behind_even_when_it_panics`). Building per check rather than once a process is what lets the folder go, as the key's note is named by the file's path; the smoke test's 12 rounds take 0.65 s

**Done when:** you approve the signed draft. Nothing is published without your go-ahead.

**You'll need:** a Developer ID, the licence decision, a trademark check on the name, and the GitHub repo if you want one.

## Bugs from the owner, 2026-11-12 (fixed alongside phase R, each with its test)

- [x] **Window controls with the sidebar hidden.** Owner: "when sidebar is hidden the traffic light buttons and sidebar toggle doesn't appear at all, they should appear when hovering over the the left corner." The 2026-11-08 rule (hide, then show on hover) only half works: they hide and never come back.
- [x] **Pages not centred.** Owner: "In some pdfs the pages aren't centered (with equal black gutters on both sides), pages area should always have equal black gutters on both sides to appear centered, with and without sidebar, and ui elements like zoom and page number shouldn't impact this."

- [x] **No way to update now from Settings.** Owner, 2026-11-12, with build 0.1.0.2 ready and waiting for five idle minutes: "I dont see the update now button in settings." The app menu's *Update to X.Y.Z…* is the only place today. Settings shows a waiting update and a button that installs it now; check too that the menu item was really there and enabled while the build waited.
- [ ] Follow-ups from the update-now merge (2026-11-12): Settings says a waiting build is ready twice (the new section and the status line under the switches): drop the status line while the section shows; the test-build verb `qa settings update.now` installs at Control, where an install is Full; a real macOS volume-access question was never exercised (the 120 s bound is proven with an opener that never returns); and on main, a local full check skips the tools tests after a merge that changed tools/, as it compares with the commit it is on (use origin/main).
- [ ] Found 2026-11-12: an update can leave a dead Launch Services record. The owner's Mac lists /Applications/.RectoPDF-update/unpacking/RectoPDF.app (registered 10:10, flags "launch-disabled no-info.plist", no identifier): the system's watcher of /Applications registered the bundle halfway through unpacking, before its Info.plist existed; the unpack then renamed it away and nothing forgot the path. It can happen on any user's Mac, by timing. Small (no identifier or document types, so not in Open With, and the registrations test filters by identifier so can't see it), but a second RectoPDF all the same. Fix: unpack outside what the system watches, or forget the path after the move, with a test that registers mid-unpack as the watcher does; then clear the owner's one stale record.
- [x] **Found 2026-11-12, installing 0.1.0.2: a development build's install hangs on opening its zip.** `recto update install <zip>` with the zip on `/Volumes/EksternalHome` didn't answer within 120 s, and `update status` said "Downloading RectoPDF 0.1.0.2…" for over five minutes. A sample showed the updater's thread inside `open()` of the zip (`recto_update::zip::unpack`, `stage.rs:259`), with macOS's `UserNotificationCenter` running: most likely the system's "access files on a removable volume" question, waiting on the owner. Fix: the app never opens a path an agent names without a bound (the `recto` process reads the zip and hands over the bytes or a copy in the app's own folder, or the open has a deadline), the command answers at once, the words say what it's waiting for, and a test with an open that never returns. Also find why the copy asks again (the release's signature against the dev builds' ad hoc one).

## Phase R: Restructuring, after the first release and before any other new feature

Owner, 2026-11-08: "add the large restructuring into the plan before we start on the other features". Owner, 2026-11-12: "The idea is that after this project is done the codebase is easily maintainable and coding projects are efficient and fast. Leading to rapid and robust development and making it easy and cheap to expand features."

The review, the target structure and each step's proof are in `docs/reviews/2026-11-12-development-speed.md`. It replaces the step list of `docs/reviews/2026-11-08-architecture.md` §4.6, whose steps 1 to 3 are done; the old numbers are in brackets. Every step is its own branch, behaviour-preserving, mergeable alone, and reports its numbers before and after. Guide §2.8 to §2.10, §5.5, §6.6 and AGENTS rule 7 hold meanwhile. The coordinator ticks these at each merge; workers don't edit this file.

**R0. Fast feedback and guards (tools only), first and all of it**
- [x] R0.1 `--quick` covers changes since the branch left `main`, runs dependents' registration guards, and a compile gate runs before a test job is queued. 1 d
- [x] R0.2 `--full` selects tests by what changed, with the app tests split into window and lifecycle tiers; CI still runs everything. Waits on the owner's answer below. 1–1.5 d
- [x] R0.3 Ratchets in `--quick`: sizes of files, functions, structs and enums that may only shrink, fixed sleeps in tests, and a hub-touch report per branch. 1 d
