- [x] R0.6 (undone 2026-11-12: three parts needed five macOS jobs a run and GitHub couldn't supply them, so CI's check is one job again, about 14.5 minutes on a branch that doesn't change the tools; under 10 minutes now waits for R1.3 and R1.4 to take tests out of the app tiers) (the three-part layout is merged, 2026-11-12; its first green run took 15.5 min of wall on a branch that changed the tools, with cold caches and macOS jobs queueing behind another branch, so under 10 minutes is not shown yet: measure main and the next two branch runs, and go back to fewer parts if five macOS jobs a run keep queueing) CI's app tests in two parallel jobs; CI under 10 minutes. 0.5 d **Closed by the prune of 2026-11-14**: CI under 10 minutes is no longer an exit criterion (owner, 2026-11-14); CI's budget in `tools/push-main.sh` and the ratchet stay as the guards.

**R0.7 One failure is enough (added 2026-11-12, before more of R1 and R2 start)**

Owner, 2026-11-12, with `main` red on CI for four pushes: "The red ci issue is something we need to fix properly. What's the solution to ensure we are able to debug and fix issues after one failure? Run targeted tests locally, reproduce errors etc". Today a CI failure cost a rerun each to learn anything: the app's messages were overwritten by the next launch, a thread count said how many and not which, the leak showed one run in three, and nothing said how to run the same thing here.
- [x] R0.7a A failure carries its evidence: any failing app test leaves one folder with every launch's messages, `qa state` before and after, the app's threads by name, a `sample` of the process, the slow seams crossed with their times, the system-pressure count, the load and the OS, and what Launch Services lists for this tree; CI uploads it. A test proves the folder is made.
- [x] R0.7b A failure says how to run it again: the failing test prints the one command that reruns it here the way CI ran it (the part, the profile, the seams and their delays as it met them), and the slow profile takes a seed so an order of arrivals can be replayed.
- [x] R0.7c CI measures a failure the first time: a failed test is run again several times in the same job with full diagnostics, the job still fails, and the log gives the rate (1 of 5, 5 of 5) with each run's evidence.
- [x] R0.7d CI's conditions here, by limits on the process (owner, 2026-11-12, asked to choose between a macOS virtual machine and process limits: "Lets do option 2"): `tools/check.sh` reruns a named test or a CI part with fewer cores, a lower priority, CI's profile and CI's concurrency. It can't give a clean user or a clean Launch Services database; failures that depend on those rest on R0.7a to R0.7c, and a virtual machine comes back to the owner only with evidence that a class of failure keeps escaping.
- [x] R0.7e `main`'s CI is watched by the tool: the push that the coordinator makes ends with a watcher that reports the run, and a red `main` makes `tools/check.sh --full` on `main` say so and refuse the next merge's stamp until it is green or the failure is marked known with a date.
R1.4 (lifecycle as state machines on a fake clock, arrivals in every order) is the structural half of this, and moves up: it starts as soon as R1.2's next slice is in.

- [x] The slow stage runs lean variants of its two longest tests (2026-11-12): 148 s to 109 s of wall. Five more tests of 21 to 30 s could get the same treatment for about 30 s more; left, as R1.4 replaces most of the stage.

  - **The independent review of the whole phase, 2026-11-14** (a reviewer given `main` at 5edeb86c and the rules, no reports; read-only, nothing run; its own confidence marks kept in the coordinator's notes). Verdict: phase R is not done by its own terms. What it built is sound where it was built (one executor with an actor, the menu-id table, typed payloads, the lifecycle and sleep-writer machines tested over every order, the bridge at 2,116 lines from about 14,000, a working ratchet), and most of the exit numbers below are missed. Findings, ranked:
    - [x] **Serious, security, fixed on `build/withheld-events` (S1):** a password-protected document's place and Find hits reach agents through `recto watch`. A drawn page is announced as `Event::PageDrawn { tab, page }` with no check for a protected tab (`core/session.rs` about 387-397, from `engine/run.rs` `page_drawn`), and the person's Find on a protected document ends in `Event::FindFinished { hits }` sent on unfiltered (`recto-control/src/finding.rs` about 142, `core/host.rs` 38-43, `Server::publish`); at Control `session show` also gives the person's Find query. The rule is applied per event constructor (`page_changed` and `FindOpened` hide it, `wait page-drawn` refuses) and two constructors miss it; the policy walks run commands and never events. Fix: one filter where events leave the session for watchers, and a walk of events (a person draws and Finds on a protected tab under a watcher; the agent's stream must not differ with that document's length or words). In 0.1.0 by its code if the events were there then: to be said by the fix's worker, for the release notes. **Done:** reproduced in process first (a watcher at Read-only over the app's own server heard `page_drawn` for each page of a five-page protected document, `page_changed` with a null page at each turn, and `find_changed` and `find_finished` with the hits of the person's Find); 0.1.0 (`v0.1.0`, dea9f10e) has the same paths by its code (the session thread's `page_drawn` through the announcer, the Finder's `find_finished` through `AppHost::listen`, the person's Find with their passwords, `session show`'s query at Control), so 0.1.0 has the fault. The fix is one boundary, `recto-control`'s `server/outlet.rs`: every published batch is a `Cleared`, made only through `Host::for_watchers` and `recto_agent::withheld::for_agents` (every `Event` classified with no default), the per-constructor checks gone; `session show` gives a protected tab no notice, zoom or layout, and no Find query while it's read (the follow-up below on its notice is done with it). Walks: `recto-app`'s `an_agent_is_told_the_same_of_two_protected_documents` over the socket, and `policy_guards`' `no_persons_command_is_heard_with_anything_of_a_protected_document`.

The person's print is a second implementation outside the executor (`ffi/printing.rs` `print_chosen` decides the freeze, the range and the selection's range, then `core/printing.rs` `start` calls the printing functions directly: no quiet-run file check, no hold on the tab, and the copy-restricted rule and its words restated at `core/printing.rs` about 311-316). Other person-facing exports also bypass `act` (`save_problem_answered`, `dismiss_selection_notice`, `set_appearance_choice`, `link_prompt_answered`, `password_answered`, `update_alert_answered`, `print_cancel`, `quit_requested`), and the coverage test sees none of them (it walks `UiAction`, menu ids and palette rows; the bridge's guard is a text search for `CoreCall::`). Fix: the print panel's answer is a `UiAction` that sends `print` as the person; every `pub fn` on `AppCore` is classified in one table (view, host input, action → command, or host-only with a reason) and the coverage test walks the table. After S2b, same files. **Done on `build/print-command` (2026-11-14):** the print panel's answer is `UiAction::Print`, which sends the person's `print --to-pdf` (a new file of the app's own in the user's temp folder, with a name nobody can work out; `--anyway` on paper, SPEC "Print to paper never asks") through `person::submit`, so the quiet run's file check, the hold on the tab, and the copy-restricted rule and its words are the executor's alone; the window watches it through `SessionAccess::print_watch`, given only to the actor at the window (the notice by the page, Stop, the tab closing, one at a time), and `printing::answered` says what came of it, a refusal before it ran included. `print_chosen` and `print_cancel` (never called by Swift) are gone from the bridge: `AppCore` 135 to 133, the bridge 2,116 to 2,092 lines. Every export is in one table (`core/headless/exports.rs`: views 60, facts from the host 53, the way in 1, the host's alone 17, each with its reason) held against the bridge's own sources in place of the `CoreCall::` text search: an export without a line fails, and so does one reaching a person's command any way but `act` and the `UiAction`s its line names (seeded in a test of its own). Of the eight: `password_answered`, `link_prompt_answered`, `save_problem_answered` and `quit_requested` are the host's alone with their reasons; `dismiss_selection_notice`, `update_alert_answered` and `set_appearance_choice` are facts from the host; `print_cancel` went. Guards first: a golden of what a person's print did on `main` (all, a range, the selection's, to a file, Stop mid-way, a second asked while one runs, a file that asks not to be copied on paper and to a file, and its Print anyway), the same after. Fixed on the way, each with a test seen failing first: a person's waiting command whose thread couldn't start said nothing (now Rust's words, and a new code `not_started`).
      - [x] (merged 2026-11-14, `build/print-protected` at 3b1f8b61, after a security review that found an agent could reopen the app's own descriptor on the print by `open /dev/fd/N`, a fix round and its re-read; the lines under this item carry what was built and what is left) **Bug, before the release, on a branch of its own (found 2026-11-14 by S2's worker; at v0.1.0 and on `main`): the person can't print a document they opened with its password.** The print's worker opens each file with no password (`DocRenderer::new(path, None, …)` in `recto-agent/src/print.rs`), so File > Print says "This PDF needs a password.", where the owner's rule is that Print ignores the flag and doesn't refuse. Design to the coordinator first, then a security review: the person's keys reach the print's worker for the person's print only, as `find_keys(actor)` does for Find, never for an agent's `print` (which stays refused on such a document, and the walk shows it); the printed file is then an unencrypted copy of a protected document for as long as it exists (0600, the unguessable name, removed on every ending), and the design says whether it should be written at all for a print to paper rather than handed to the system's printing in memory. Not started.
        - **Design done 2026-11-14 (read-only, by an Opus designer; accepted by the coordinator; not built: it waits for `build/withheld-compare` to leave `recto-agent`).** (1) Keys go only through a door the server can't reach: an inherent `SessionHost::run_for_person` with the actor fixed, the only place a `Hosted` holding the keys is built; what the server calls (`Host::run`/`check`) never has keys whatever actor it is given, and the actor rule stays as a second lock; Find moves onto the same door. A key is looked up by the open file's identity, not its path, and only for pages from the tab's own file (a page added from another protected file gets none, as on screen). (2) The person's print never has a name on disk: created 0600 and unlinked before the first byte, its descriptor handed to the window, so the kernel frees it on every ending, a crash and a kill included; for every print of a person's, protected or not. First proof: whether CoreGraphics opens `/dev/fd/N` (else Swift maps the descriptor). (3) The two land in one branch: keys with today's named file would leave a plain named copy of a protected document after a crash, which 0.1.0 doesn't have, as there the print fails. (4) Found: the copy-restricted check fails open on a password file (it can't read the flag without the key and answers "not restricted"); latent today, live with keys; `copy_restricted_with_password` is part of the branch. (5) Accepted and to be documented: `session check` says `busy: printing` for as long as a print takes, and an agent's exports wait behind it (timing only). To check in the build: what reconcile publishes during a print (the twins walk holds it), `qa print-to-pdf` on a protected shown tab in the walk, whether the print job's title was read with the password. Ten tests named in the design, one assertion joined to the existing real-app print launch. Estimate 1.5 to 2 worker-days on Opus, then a security review. **For the owner:** may a person Save as PDF or Open in Preview (the system's print panel) a document they opened with its password, as an unencrypted file, with only the copy flag's "Print anyway" step? The rules don't say; recommended yes (decided 2026-11-15: allowed). The S2 follow-up (6) below is stale: the file is 0600 by the code and its test.
        - **Built on `build/print-protected` (2026-11-14, waiting for its security review):** the unnamed file first (no commit has keys reaching a named file), then the door (`SessionHost::run_for_person`, `AppHost::run_for_person`; the window's print watch moved onto it too, found on the way), then the keys in the print (`key_for`, own pages by path, the opened file's identity) with the copy flag read with the key; `/dev/fd/N` opens in CoreGraphics (mapped; a 33 MB two-thread probe passed), so no bridge or Swift change.

Built as recommended: yes, no step of its own; the place a notice would go is marked "FOR THE OWNER" in `core/printing.rs`'s `chosen`.

        - **The security review's S1, fixed on the branch (2026-11-14):** `/dev/fd/N` opened the print's descriptor again, for an agent at Control, as a plain tab or a page's source. The file wall (`recto_platform::document_file`) now takes every open of a document by its path: `/dev` refused before anything is looked at, a file with no link after the open; a scan holds the rest to a reviewed list.
        - [ ] **For the owner (SF1, accepted residual):** macOS has no `O_TMPFILE`, so the print's file has a name for the instant between its making and its unlinking. A process running as the user that watches the temp folder and opens it then reads everything the print writes, and can try at every print; the threat list's reason for setting same-user processes aside (they could read the PDFs directly) doesn't hold for a protected file. Nothing through the app's CLI reaches it. Written in `docs/engineering/opening.md`.
        - [ ] F3, the window's side: whether CoreGraphics' own open of `/dev/fd/N` is close-on-exec can only be seen in the real app, and the existing protected-print launch has no cheap read of the app's descriptors; `spawn_detached` (std's `Command`) would hand a child any descriptor that isn't. Either a QA read of the app's descriptors with no link (by `proc_pidinfo`) joined to that launch, or `spawn_detached` made to close every descriptor it isn't given (`POSIX_SPAWN_CLOEXEC_DEFAULT`, as the render workers' spawn does).
        - [ ] **F-A (the re-read, 2026-11-14):** `keep_backup` (`recto-core/src/save.rs`) resolves the tab's own path and then clones or copies the original **by path**, after the walled open and the whole plan, so a same-user process that swaps the tab's file for a link in that window is stopped today only by accident (`realpath` of an unlinked descriptor names a path that doesn't exist): the backup should clone or copy from the descriptor the wall admitted (`clone_open_file`, and a copy from the open file).
        - [ ] CoreGraphics' own descriptor of the print (`/dev/fd/N`, opened in Swift): its close-on-exec flag isn't verified in the real app (F3's window side, above).
        - [ ] F4: whether a print goes "to a file" is decided in Swift from the job's disposition, so a PDF-workflow service other than Save as PDF or Open in Preview may skip "Print anyway" on a copy-restricted document: a rule in Swift, to move to Rust (the host says the disposition; Rust decides what it asks).
        - [ ] F2: a key goes by device and inode, so a hard link made at the tab's own path to another protected file whose key the person typed prints that file to the person's own printer (by the person's own act, to their own printer; nothing reaches an agent).
        - [ ] F5 (QA only): a stale `finished(path)` (a path that isn't the print handed out) empties `qa_target`, the QA print's place to publish to.
        - [ ] **Product fault, found by this branch's 20 rounds like CI (2026-11-14), its own branch after `recto-render-service/src/host.rs` is split (it's over its line budget):** a print's or an export's fresh worker killed at the 5 s page deadline during its Open on a starved machine says "This file took too long to open." and isn't tried again, where a busy text read is (`MAX_BUSY_READS`). Evidence: `the_persons_print_of_a_password_opened_document_prints` failed 1 of 20 under `--like-ci` (its second print's Open, 17 s for the round), at load 3.5 to 7.3; main's `a_persons_print_does_what_it_did` passed 20 of 20 at once after. 0.1.0 has it by its code: `open_in` counts a starved open as `too_busy` and fails, and nothing that prints or exports tries again. Fix: a starved open (the worker's CPU under half the deadline) tried once more with a new worker. The branch's tests that open encrypted files in fresh workers take the long test deadline meanwhile (`page_deadline_for_this_process`), as the comparison walks do.

- [x] Found 2026-11-13, CI run 43401013894 on `1f2aeb9`: an agent's `back` straight after a Find jump left the page where it was (1 of 5). The page view reports the page it moved to, and the session thread's look took that report as the reader's own scroll, over a step made since: a jump with a place on the page, or Find's reveal carried out after the next command, undid the step. A person could do it: a link to partway down a page, then ⌘[ within a frame. Fixed: what a page view says of where it is is taken only when it's the view's own (`recto_app::core::places::is_views_own`: the view has been sent everything the session has for its tab, and no jump is waiting), in one function for a person and an agent; Find's reveal brings its hit into view only while the tab is still on the hit's page. Held by tests that hold the session thread between two calls and fail every time on the old code; the CI test passed 200 of 200. In hand, the same family: a Find reveal waiting for its page's text survives a later jump (the page view); a same-page jump still waiting can't be seen off the session thread.

Not a regression: the tree before the page view's regrouping shows the same, the counting code is unchanged, and at ordinary load both pass (9 runs each). "No bare frame" was asserted of every wake with no patience in the product, so it held only when the renderer or a sleep picture was ready before the host's second apply. In hand: the wake's report says what it had to show, and the test holds that a wake with a picture covering the view is never bare. **Open, and possibly a real weakness:** three wakes from a sleep file were bare for about 250 ms under load with no previews attached; whether they had a picture, and where it was, is being read. (done: `build/wake-blank-frames`, 4d8676cf: a sleep file carries only pictures at its place, and a wake's report says what it had to show)

Done 2026-11-13: `tools/ratchet.sh --resolve` reads the three versions from the index, writes the merged baseline and lists each key the sides differed on; it does not stage the file, so `tools/ratchet.sh` is run on the merged tree first. Seven tests on real conflicted merges.
- [x] Found 2026-11-13, CI run 43411305187 on `82b05457`: `forward_comes_back_to_a_jump_on_the_same_page_not_yet_shown` failed 4 of 5 on CI and never here. Two causes, both merged the same day (`build/forward-fix`): a jump sent in the same look that shows its tab in the pane anew lost how far down the page it went (the pane was shown at the tab's saved place; `send_jump` in `reconcile.rs` now says it once for both branches, with a forcing test seen failing with CI's message), and the headless kit's `hold_session_thread` returned before the session thread had stopped, so a look could land between a step's two halves (0 failures in 21 runs after, 2 in 71 before; not proven by a forced test).
- [x] Found 2026-11-13 (the same worker): outside the kit the ordering is real. An agent's `back`, `forward` or `goto` takes the views' places and then runs its step under two separate session locks; a look in between takes the waiting jump, and the step marks the place the view had before the jump. In hand on `build/sent-jump` after the menu table; `finding::an_agent_jumps_to_a_hit_chooses_one_and_opens_find` failed once under load the same day (`where` said page 10 after `find --hit 2` answered page 11) and is checked against the same fix. The forced orderings (2026-11-13) all give page 11 and it passed 150 of 150 runs; not reproduced, its first output wasn't kept. Still open. Built on `build/sent-jump` and sent back by its independent review: a sent jump outlived the person's own scroll on the same page (a link down the page, a scroll back up, then ⌘[ kept the link's place), because only a look forgot it and a scroll within a page queues none. Ruled: order by when the read was made (the engine's sequence with each read; a newer read wins, whoever made it). Lands after the menu table. Merged 2026-11-13 after its second review found nothing that blocks: each read of a view's place carries a stamp from one place clock that every move of a place ticks; a read older than the last taken in for a tab is skipped; a sent jump is forgotten by any read made at or after its send. The test that had hidden the first attempt's fault (a settle that made a look the app never makes) was rewritten. `Session` 192 to 190 methods; scrolling allocates what it did (56 in 50 steps).
  - [ ] Left by its second review: while panes are reordered a tab can be hot in two engines for an instant, and the old one's read can tell of a just-sent jump (take a hot place only from the pane the session has the tab in, as moved pages already require, `places.rs`); the hot tab is read under another lock than the stamped places (return it from `Engine::places`).
- [x] Found 2026-11-13: `core::headless::exits::every_edit_survives_a_kill` failed once in a quick check: "waited 20s for every tab's edits in its journal; under way: []". Merged the same day (`build/kill-journal-wait`): `ensure_private_dir` made a folder open to others and only then narrowed it, so of two threads making `session/journals/` at once (the saver's writer and the sleep files' writer) one could refuse the folder as not private and fail its journal write; the edit then waited 2 s more, and a kill in those 2 s lost it. In 0.1.0, about once in a state folder's life. The folder is now made private in one call, and the kit's wait fails at once with the store's error and each tab's missing journal. The ordering was forced and gave the failure's exact text; the original run's own state wasn't recovered.
- [x] Blank frames on wake, 2026-11-13 (`build/wake-blank-frames`, merged): a sleep file kept the pictures of an older place when the tab was left at a place nothing was drawn at, so a wake loaded pictures it couldn't show. A sleep file now carries only pictures of the pages at its place (`SleepMaterial::in_view`, `sleep_writer::with_older_pictures`), and a wake's report says what it had to show. Not shown: the moment a tab is left with nothing drawn at its place (a probe never fired in 18 runs under load).
- [ ] **Unsaved edits when the file changes on disk: a page matcher** (owner, 2026-11-13: "we need to run a page matcher of some kind. That's the way we can ensure edits on the correct page"; `docs/OWNER-RULES.md`). Step 1, in hand: what happens today, by reading and by tests (a rotation on page 9 and the new version has 5 pages; a page inserted before the edited one; the file overwritten in place while it is read; a tab showing and untouched when its file changes). Step 2: the design, reported first: what a page is matched by (recorded when the edit is made, a few bytes in the journal, since the old bytes may be gone), what an unmatched or doubly matched page does, what is said to the person, and that an agent's edits follow the same rule. Built after the design is agreed; sooner if step 1 shows edits landing on the wrong page today. Step 1 done 2026-11-13, by reading (two existing tests run; the rest read or inferred, as marked in the report): an edit names its page by its index in the file as it was, and nothing of a page's content is recorded; when the file changes the list is kept as it is and the notice says pages may not line up, so every edit shows on whatever page is at that index now; Save is refused, so nothing wrong reaches a file, but for one rare path (a rewrite in place that keeps size, time and inode). Found on the way, in hand on `build/changed-file-edits` as bugs, tests first: Save As is refused too while the notice and the agent's hint say it keeps both, so the edits can only be discarded; a file replaced between a read's stamp and its read, or an edit made before a changed file's read lands, drops the list and deletes its journal while the notice says it was kept (inferred, to be forced); an edited tab's renderer can stay on "this file changed" for good; every look at such a tab reads the file's structure again and resets the contents folds; Find's hits on it are never found again. Step 2, the matcher's design, is with the owner. The bug fixes (`build/changed-file-edits`: edits and their journal no longer deleted, true words, the stuck page view, the repeated work, Find) passed their checks and were sent back by their independent review on 2026-11-13: an edited tab whose stamp had been dropped still counted as current, so text, render, select, search and print-to-PDF used the other version's pages with nothing refusing; the state was kept only as a notice other notices overwrite; a second change in a row kept the middle version's contents; and a different file of the same size, time and page count renamed into place is taken for the earlier version whatever its inode. In round one of the fixes (Opus). Merged 2026-11-13 after two review rounds, the second finding nothing that blocks: whether a tab's unsaved edits fit its file is one fact (`EditsFit`: fits; the file changed and the earlier version is known; changed and not known; the saved edits don't fit this file), read through `session.notices().fit(id)` and never from a notice's text; one rule (`recorded_version_is_current`) is asked by every reader and writer of a document's pages, so text, render, select, export, print, snapshot, search, index, Find, contents, signatures, sleep files, Save and Save As are refused when the edits don't fit; a second change in a row is news; a look made before a save landed changes nothing. In 0.1.0: edits and their journal could be deleted in a narrow window while the tab said they were kept; edits on a changed file could be saved nowhere while the words promised Save As; a page view could stay on "this file changed" for good. Still so until the matcher: on screen the page view and the sidebar show the new file's pages at the edits' positions, and the person's Copy takes the text of the page shown; a different file of the same size, time and page count renamed into place is taken for the earlier version; pages another program appended can't be reached under edits.
  - [x] Merged 2026-11-13 from `build/changed-file-followups` (Opus), from the second review: `activate` with an edit list that couldn't be woken takes the new file as the edits' own (a later Save could append onto it: rule 5; the code predates the branch); a `Same` look that clears the record of another version isn't counted as news; undoing to clean on a tab whose edits don't fit leaves it saying edits are kept, with redo able to bring them back; after a restart Find and search skip such a tab's pages without a reason until a look lands; three sentences false in rare states (a read error said as a change; Save As keeps both said of a Save As just refused; unknown said as doesn't fit); the notice-code scan cut each file at its first `#[cfg(test)]`; the stale-look guard untested at two of its three call sites. All fixed, each test seen failing first; a fifth state (`VersionUnknown`: an edited tab whose version was never known) and two errors of their own (`changed_while_copying`, `file_unreadable`); the restart window closed with no change to the record's format; `SaveError` moved out to `recto-core/src/save_error.rs` (`save.rs` 2,271 lines to 2,105).

For the matcher: its map must feed the page view and the selection as well as Save, Find and search.
  - [ ] **The page matcher (design agreed by the owner, 2026-11-13; built after phase R and before the release)** (its page identity is also the locator's, phase 8.1: built once, for both) (recommended by the coordinator and matched by the fixing worker's notes): per edit list, at the first edit, record in the journal header what tells versions apart cheaply (the PDF's trailer `/ID` pair and the page count) and, per page an edit touches, its object id and a small digest of its content; match by id checked against the digest, else by digest aligned in order; an edit whose page isn't found is parked, kept and said, never applied to another page, and blocks Save until discarded; the list is re-based on the new file as `rebase_on_saved` does. It also closes the same-size-and-time case and reaches appended pages.
    - Built on `build/page-matcher` (2026-11-15), as designed in its step 0 and agreed by the coordinator and the owner the same day (owner's answers in `docs/OWNER-RULES.md`; 0.1.1 ships after slices 1 and 2, slice 3 after the release). **Slice 1 (nothing visible):** the digest `recto-page/1`, defined once in `recto-core/src/page_id.rs` with golden vectors (made pages, and page 1 of three corpus files), read a piece at a time with every path under its 64 MiB page cap (the research sketch's stored-bytes path had none), the frame tag beside it; which pages a list's edits name (`recto-session/src/edit/identity.rs`: each step's run, the pages either side, and their neighbours in the file; capped at 8,192 a list, about 36 bytes each), read by one app-wide thread started when needed (`recto-app/src/core/page_ids.rs`, counted in what's running) while the file is the edits' version, never for a password-opened tab, and kept beside the journal in a sidecar (the line below).
    - Slice 1 after its independent review (2026-11-15, the same branch): identities left the journal for `pages-<key>.ids` beside it (B1: journals byte for byte as on `main`; written once per page read, tied to the journal's name, file version and page count, removed on every path its journal goes by and swept at a launch; 0.1.0 and the carry check match none of it); `recto-page/1` fixed for good (count framing, only what the content draws with `Do`, no identity past a cap but an image past its own cap, which is its first 16 MiB, stored length and geometry; 256 drawn XObjects; golden vectors on made files, run on CI; 15 of the corpus's 5,872 pages have no identity); stored lengths charged before a read or a decrypt (S1); jobs bounded by bytes, a give-up remembered for the version, a look after restore, the opened file checked by its own descriptor; `page_decode` and `sidecar` fuzzed.
    - Slice 1 after its second review (2026-11-15, the same branch): a form whose content fails to decode has no children (F1); a byte budget of 2 GiB per file version closes a list, and a page past a cap marks only itself, so a large page named as a neighbour no longer turns following off for its file (F2; a named page the page tree doesn't have is tried too); ASCII85 decodes no further than asked and `decode.rs` says each decoder's unit, a predictor's whole row and a RunLength whole run (F3); the frame tag's low two bits are the page's own quarter turns (`frame_turns`), with the sidecar's format at version 2; a journal starting again under another name gets its sidecar whole; a sidecar that can't be written (the tab's version gone unknown) no longer holds the tab from going to disk; the open by path alone is gone; the scan's names looked up in a sorted index; golden vectors for the failed form, names with `#` and an inline image. Corpus, release, load 3: 5,872 pages in 4.1 s of CPU, 15 without identity, peak footprint 19.1 MiB.

Undo ends at a followed change; where both reordered, the person's placement wins. Screenshots of the notice, light and dark, joined to an existing launch.
      - Built on `build/page-matcher-2` (2026-11-15), handed back for its independent review: the pure matcher (`recto-session/src/edit/matcher.rs`) and rebase (`edit/rebase.rs`), with the table test (`edit/rebase/table.rs`: 30 ways a file changes × turn, delete, move, insert, highlight, a file highlight taken out); the session's part (`version_change.rs`: a job read off the lock on slice 1's thread, taken in under it); the notice `edits_followed` (never in a record: 0.1.0 can't read it), `edits`'s `followed` and the `edits_followed` event (counts only).
      - The coordinator's rulings of 2026-11-15 on what the design left open: the pages that must be found are those the rebase uses (each edit's page and the page a moved or added run is placed by), and neighbours only tell copies apart; a scan cut at its caps (200,000 pages, 2 GiB hashed or read) matches nothing by digest; "moved" is out of the longest run still in the file's order, a run goes after the fixed page before it, or before the first one at the start, and with no fixed page the list is held; redo goes with undo, and unsaved highlights on pages the person deleted go too (unreachable once undo ends; never saved); another inode with the edits' size and time is another version (closes the same-size rename case); the notice clears when the person leaves the tab; a follow is tried when a read of the tab finds a known other version, once for that version.
      - The cost of closing the rename case (ruled 2026-11-15): a file put back from a backup with the same bytes under unsaved edits is now said as changed by another program and the undo history ends there, where before it fitted silently (a version written back in place, same inode, still fits silently with its history). Keeping the history when nothing moved (every page the edits use at its own number, the length the same) is for slice 3, with the owner's word, as "the undo history ends at a change the edits followed" is their decision.

Accepted for 0.1.1. While the kept list is held (its file changed), the notice is behind "changed on disk", as the two-tab one is.
      - [ ] `.not-merged` files are never pruned, bounded only by crashes times a journal's size cap: a count or an age cap.
      - [ ] After a crash takeover the record's reading page and trail are the old list's positions, not mapped: `rebase::moved_position` needs the follow's positions, which no sidecar keeps (it would mean keeping the mapping beside the identities).

- [x] Draw before a tab sleeps (owner, 2026-11-13: "I think it makes sense, and extra draw is not costly right?"; `docs/OWNER-RULES.md`). In hand on `build/draw-before-dormant` (Opus). Decided by the coordinator on the worker's reading: drawn when the tab leaves the screen, not at dormancy, because a worker exits after 30 s idle and a tab goes dormant after 300 s, so at dormancy there is almost never a worker to draw with. Into `left_pictures` under its 4 MiB cap, after the shown tab's own work, never under memory pressure, one write per dormancy as today. Numbers owed: CPU, footprint, bytes written, before and after. Built and measured (worker CPU 7 to 21 ms a draw, one write as before, wake from a picture in under 0.5 ms where it was bare for 42 to 78 ms, 0 of 119 leaves in the long session), then sent back by its independent review: a draw could start a worker after all (a worker past 64 MiB is retired and a fresh one opens the file again; a page needing the heavy class took the shared heavy slot), and a region job for the tab just left was no longer cancelled at the switch. In round one of the fixes (Opus): a no-start flag through the renderer, with tests on a real worker. Merged 2026-11-13 after two review rounds and a last narrow fix: a sleep job runs in a mode in which the renderer starts, reopens, retires into or escalates to no worker, reset however the job ends; a refused answer is never remembered as an answer (on dark paper a look decided without the page's image boxes had been kept: a large photo's page came back light grey, a small photo a negative); a sleep file carries pictures only when its tab's pages are the pages they were made from (in 0.1.0: an agent's edit of a tab off screen, then sleep, showed the old page's picture for about 45 ms at wake). Long session after the fixes, one run at a load of 26 falling to 17: 0 of 119 leaves owed pages, 60 of 60 wakes from a sleep file, first frame 1.5 ms at the median, 0 blank frames, the app flat at about 17.4 MiB. Numbers in `docs/engineering/sleep.md`. Not seen on screen by anyone yet.

- [x] Found 2026-11-13 (R3.5's worker): `recto-headings` `it_goes_when_its_stdin_closes` failed once under load: the contents helper, given the form bomb and its stdin closed, worked on for 5.3 s to the end. Merged the same day (`build/helper-goes`): both of the helper's ways out (stdin closed, memory limit passed) called `std::process::exit`, which runs exit handlers and destructors and can be held by them (forced: with a stalled handler neither stopped it in 5 s); they now leave through `recto_platform::leave::leave_now` (`_exit`), and a helper whose watchers can't be started refuses the job where it ran without them. In 0.1.0; the app also kills the helper from outside on cancel, time and memory, so the harm was bounded. The one failure itself wasn't reproduced in 40 runs.
- [x] Found 2026-11-13: two or three full checks reaching their app-test stages together take the machine to a load of 100 to 150 on 10 cores (the system's own launch daemons at the top), an app tier that takes about 130 to 220 s alone then runs at about 40 tests an hour, and lifecycle tests fail starved. In hand on `build/one-app-tier`: the stages that launch the app take one lock across this repo's worktrees. Merged 2026-11-13 (`build/one-app-tier`): one lock, a directory in the shared git folder, held from the app tests through the slow stage and registrations and by a `--like-ci` run of an app test; a waiter says who holds it, a dead holder's lock is taken over, a wait is bounded (45 minutes, its own exit code, "Nothing was tested") and isn't counted in the stage's seconds. The larger part of that afternoon's load turned out to be 30 busy loops a worker had started to starve a test and left running for 42 minutes; `docs/agent-instructions/worker-testing.md` now forbids a worker's own background load.
- [x] `tools/push-main.sh`: two watchers alive at once (a push made while the last one's CI is still watched) each record the same green run, so the kept times hold it twice and the median is wrong. A run is recorded once. (done: `build/push-main-watch`, 04f9a692: a run id is recorded once)
- [x] Found 2026-11-13 (the draw worker, once, at a load of 87 to 102): `exits::every_edit_survives_a_quit` failed with "the headless app didn't get the record's lock" when the app started again after the quit. Whether a writer thread of the quit can still hold the store after `end()` returns isn't known; the real app's relaunch after an update takes the same lock. Seen a second time the same day from another test (`every_edit_survives_sleep_and_a_kill_while_asleep`, load about 20), then found and merged (`build/record-lock-relaunch`): a file lock belongs to the open file description, and a child being started by `posix_spawn` on another thread (the agreement look's render worker) holds a copy of every descriptor until its exec has finished, so closing the lock's descriptor at that instant left the record locked for the next start (a probe here: 136 times in 200,187 closes; 0 with an unlock first). `let_go` now unlocks and then closes, a dropped store the same; a store that has let go never takes the record back (a second fault found on the way: an ended app's straggler could take it from the next one); and a start that doesn't get the record says who has it. The real app isn't affected in practice: a process can't finish exiting in the middle of a spawn, and the installer waits for the old one to be gone.
  - [ ] Left by it: the control server's lock (`recto-control/src/server.rs:135`), the installer's (`recto-update/src/install.rs:245`) and the corpus lock (`recto-index/src/corpus.rs`) are released by a close alone and can be held up the same way: an unlock before each close.
  - [x] (merged 2026-11-14 00:40, `build/record-lock-retry`, a day ahead of its slipped date; three independent reads, each of which found a way to lose a session or roll back a good update before it merged, and the coordinator's read of the last round's machine) **Decided by the owner, 2026-11-13 (yes, retry: quietly for about ten seconds, then remembered once it has the lock):** a launch that doesn't get the record's lock within 600 ms starts without the restored tabs, says "Another RectoPDF is keeping your session's state, so what you do in this window won't be remembered", and never asks for the lock again. Whether it should try again later, so a window opened a moment too early starts being remembered.
    - As merged: a launch waits once for the lock; without it, `Trying` (a try a second on its own short thread, 10 s, or 60 s for a launch an update may have opened) → `Restoring` (patience 5 s) → `Keeps`/`NotKept`; one pure machine (`lifecycle::keeping`) whose step returns what to do and one carrier that does all of it, walked over every state and event (the updater told exactly once, the notice as the state says); writers go by `keeping::may_write` (holds the lock and keeps the record), before the saver's gate and under it; agents get only looks while it tries or restores; the window is made again once for a late keep if untouched (asserted in the real app with shots); a second copy an update opened writes `launch-ok` without a tab count so the installer sees it started. `Lifecycle` 296 → 344 bytes (one an app).

- [x] Dark paper, the background decides (owner, 2026-11-13: "The image should flip if the background is mostly white"; `build/dark-paper-background`, merged 2026-11-13 after two review rounds and a last fix read by the coordinator). Kept pictures large enough to judge covering under half the page (their union): the page turns over as before; at or above half: the background outside them decides (mostly white: turned over with the picture kept; edge to edge, coloured or dark: dimmed). Of 1,291 surveyed pages 8 change, all from dimmed to turned over with the picture kept (the UN SDG covers, NASA and USGS figure pages). A page's look no longer depends on which draw came first (0 of 1,291 across three picture sizes; whether one image on a page turns over can still differ across a sleep and wake on 7 of 292 image pages, 28 at the lowest resolution: accepted, no extra draw and nothing kept in a cold tab). Image boxes from a worker are put the right way round at decode (a crafted page had reported 21.8 pages of paper); past 64 images boxes that touch are folded and past 64 still apart the page is judged whole; the walk's kills don't count toward stopping the document and have their own cap of 16. The decision p50 264 → 270 µs for the look picture (load 15 to 54); the walk on a low-resolution page within noise (5.19 → 5.02 ms made page; 381.9 → 376.9 ms USGS sheet, main emulated on one build).

Dark: at least half of the background (everything but kept pictures, white-backed pictures read as they are) darker than luma 128, and at least half of the page's ring (the outer twentieth of each side) dark too, so a dark slide or cover stays dark and a white page with a dark band, sidebar, vector chart or code listing turns over. Of 1,291 surveyed pages one changes against `main` (the USGS black cover); 0 differ across picture sizes. Every page is now decided, pages with no images too: from the preview, a tile that is the whole page, or a 128 px picture (after a wake, zoomed out past a preview, looks forgotten, or a preview only at lower resolution); a look that can't be decided yet is held quietly and tried again at 3 s, three times. Decision 10 to 16 µs for a page with no images (load 5 to 13). The vector drawing stays as built.

- [x] Faults a measurement run turned up (merged 2026-11-14 02:55, `build/measured-faults`; two review rounds and the coordinator's read of the last fixes). **Security, in 0.1.0 by its code, for the release notes:** a typed password was used for any path the keyring had a key for, so with a crafted PDF (lopdf reads it as plain, PDFium asks a password) whose password the person had typed, a page added from it to another document was drawn there, was on screen for an agent, was found by the person's Find and was pictured in that document's sleep file; and after the protected tab closed while such a document was open, reopening the file showed it without asking and unmarked. Now a typed password is used only for the tab it was typed for (page view, links, Find) and ends with it (the keyed worker is let go; a reopened file asks again); such a page says it isn't shown here; sleep files of the older format are dropped at first read (one bare wake a tab, once). **Also in 0.1.0:** a file anyone may open (encrypted with an empty user password: many government and UN reports) never got a sleep file and always woke bare (what keeps pages out of a sleep file is that a password was needed, not that the file is encrypted); the page view parked for good when the system refused a surface (now tried again at 1 s doubling to 8); a comparison pane let go mid-draw leaked its waiting tiles' surfaces for the app's life (an estimate of 8 to 32 MiB a pane). Not faults: the first tap after a rest "taking 280 to 300 ms" was the measurement waiting for the page number's quarter-second fade (the tap is 6 to 9 ms); 7 workers for 8 warm tabs is a worker over 64 MiB let go 3 s after its last job, by design. The headless kit now releases the surfaces it is handed (it leaked every one: 1.04 MiB an app start to 0.03 in a start-draw-quit loop: very likely the "kit grows about 11.6 MiB per app start" item).
  - [x] One more of the same, found when `a_file_opened_again_after_the_tab_its_password_was_typed_for_closed_asks_for_it_again` failed once in a full check (merged 2026-11-14 04:25, `build/keyless-prompt`): a renderer given a typed password was kept while any held tab had that file as its own, and the page view lets renderers go only between jobs, so the crafted file opened again while another tab's job ran as its own tab closed was drawn with the old tab's password, unasked and unmarked. Now a keyed renderer serves only the tab it was keyed for (`OpenDoc::serves`). Forced with a seam, failing every time before. 0.1.0 has it in a narrower form (reopened within one job of the close), by its code.
  - [ ] Remaining load/privacy follow-ups: an agent's `select`, `copy` and `extract` on a page added from a password file use `needs_password` rather than `page_unreadable`; check a Find already running when its tab's key ends; hardware that refuses a surface for a reason other than exhaustion is retried every 8 s indefinitely. The 0.1.0 routes were read, not run. Closed portions: Find text is encrypted and disk page pictures removed in 0.1.1; protected-file Save and Save As are disabled. Other retained document data and the explicit-state disk-key exception are separate issues, not claims that the normal corpus remains plaintext.
- [x] Sleep files: a tab wakes with its page, and the cool-down (merged 2026-11-14 02:10, `build/sleep-picture-later-cycles`; two review rounds, a focused third read of the agents' share, the coordinator's read of the last fixes). Fixed, each with a test that failed first, and each in 0.1.0 by the code: a tab left at another page inside the 60 s write gap woke to an empty pane (ordinary tab switching with more than three tabs); a tab whose worker outlived its sleep was never checked again and got no new sleep file (the test that failed 1 in 5 on CI); a tab closed, saved or changed while its sleep file was queued left the file on disk (pictures of up to 12 pages and the contents titles, till the 30-day or 256 MiB prune); old contents written under a new version's stamp. Built on the owner's decisions of 2026-11-13 (`docs/OWNER-RULES.md`): a pushed-out tab's file waits 20 s in the queue and is handed back from memory if the tab is shown again (about 30% fewer writes in a scripted hour; about 3 MiB more held in the app while flicking, measured on release builds); sleep files may take 16 MiB an hour, agents' doing at most 2 of them by any route (measured before written), three tabs stay warm. No sleep file is written by a copy that doesn't keep the record (`keeping::may_write`). `Inner` 5,064 bytes (budget raised from 4,880 with the owner's leave to edit the guard, 2026-11-14).
- [x] Five tests failing under load, 2026-11-14 (`build/load-failures`): three page-view tests at load 15 and 1 run in 20 under `--like-ci` on `main`, and two real-app restore tests at load 77, had one cause. The kernel emptied surfaces the page view had marked discardable (the machine was down to 3 purgeable pages), at moments no test chose; forcing that purge reproduced each one's numbers every run. Decided by the coordinator: a seen page coming back blank for the moment its preview takes to draw again under real memory pressure is the design (rule 1). So in the reader's tests and the headless kit the system takes only when a test says (`cache::let_system_take`, `Engine::qa().system_takes_only_when_told()`, a release app has no switch), and the two real-app tests force the purge (`qa memory-pressure discard`) inside their launches. **A product fix, in 0.1.0 by its code:** a sleep picture made from the tiles once the system has taken the page's preview left out tiles of one colour, so a one-colour page had no picture (the next launch on bare paper) and a band of colour came out as paper. **What a purge costs at a flush, decided:** the picture of a page in view whose preview the OS took is rebuilt from the tiles, so the next flush (a quit, a hand-off) may rewrite the tab's sleep file once though nothing about the document changed, counted against the hour's cap like any other write: 5,179 to 5,275 bytes in the real app, 615 to 614 in the kit. That a quit with nothing changed writes nothing is held in process (`a_quit_with_nothing_changed_writes_nothing`). A sixth, the same day, from the same purge: `recto-reader::engine a_replaced_file_gets_its_pages_looked_at_again_on_dark_paper` read [0, 0, 0] from a surface off its layer; the crate's tests in `tests/` now have the same switch, through its own `qa` feature as a dev-dependency, and a binary of their own (`tests/marking.rs`) that never turns it on holds that the app still marks what leaves the layers. Two more failures of the day had other causes, each fixed with a test that failed first: `life_script` read the updater before it had been told of a launch held back for the record's lock, as the app's settled didn't count that telling (it does now, by a guard a panic can't strand); and `per_tab_work`'s count moved with tabs going dormant on the real clock and with a page's word landing in its window (it now counts the action's own call and its look, with drawing held and dormancy put off).

- [x] **Security, in 0.1.0 as released. Found 2026-11-13 by R2.4's review and its worker's reading:** a quiet run starts at Full (`recto-cli/src/qa.rs:70`), release builds accept `--qa`, and `open` takes any absolute path (`recto-agent/src/commands/tabs.rs:278`). So an agent on a machine whose copy is at Off can start a quiet run on temp state and then open, read and rewrite the user's own PDFs with ordinary commands, at a level the user never gave. Fix in hand on `build/quiet-run-files` (Opus, design reported first): in a quiet run nothing reaches a file outside the run's own folders, by any route, stated once. Owner, 2026-11-13, asked whether it goes out as 0.1.1 at once: "no lets wait until we are done with R before we release". It merges and waits for the release after phase R. Merged 2026-11-13 (`build/quiet-run-files`) after three reads by an independent security reviewer, the last finding nothing that blocks: every session on a state folder set with `RECTO_STATE_DIR` reaches no file outside the system's temp folders (never `$TMPDIR`), for reads and writes; checked for every path a command names, from the table, and again in the session wherever a tab's path is handed out (`Tabs::path_of`), on replayed history, and in the installer; `qa run` reads scripts only from temp and never quotes them, `$NAME` expands only `RECTO_QA_*`, `qa start --app` starts only a RectoPDF bundle. Held by two walks that run every command and every QA verb with an outside file in each value and assert it unmoved, unread and unwritten (four seeded gaps each fail them). A real run pays no extra lock. Limits said in SPEC.md: the temp folders are shared with other apps; a folder swapped for a link after a check; hard links; for a new file the gap is as long as its draw. Waits for the release after phase R, by the owner's word.

- [ ] **For the owner to decide.** Found 2026-11-13 (R2.3's worker, by probe): a PDF opened with its password can't be saved. The writer reads the file without its password, so both the appending and the rewriting save refuse it as damaged and write nothing (the file is untouched and still protected). Until now the window's Save failed with the agents' protected refusal as its words. From `build/one-executor`: Save and Save As are off for such a tab, with "This PDF is protected by a password, and RectoPDF can't save into a protected file yet, so Save and Save As are off.", and the close prompt offers Cancel and Don't Save. To decide: (1) saving into a protected file, re-encrypted with the same password and permissions, by the lopdf writer (rule 5), as a feature; (2) meanwhile, whether edits should be offered at all on a tab that can't be saved; (3) whether Save As to an unprotected copy is ever wanted (it removes a password, so it would need the same plain words a signed file's copy gets).
- [x] Found 2026-11-13, CI run 43369527265 on `8e13c72`: the registrations stage found this checkout's own build folder registered. `a_quiet_run_refuses_the_real_state` ran the build folder's binary itself and left it registered 20 of 20 by hand; it runs a copy now, and a guard reads the test sources so none starts that binary directly. It isn't shown to be what failed on CI: nearly every app test launches the build folder's app by `qa start --app` and forgets it once with no look, so a late registration can come from any of about 180 tests and none can be named. With the owner's go-ahead (2026-11-13, "Yes, lets do it like this"), for that one path the stage forgets it until it stays gone (two looks), says so in a line with how long it took, and fails only if a process of it still runs or it won't stay gone; a test's own copy and anything else of the checkout still fail the stage. Not exercised: those two failure paths. If a stray comes from yet another source, the owner is told before the stage is changed again.

Any tool or test that kills a fresh copy and forgets it once has the same hole.

Needs: a line in the app's messages for each file a quit writes and why (in recto-app), then a hunt with the exploring run.

Measure quitting many large open documents and the actual session-recovery gap. An explicit unlock after the final durable write needs its own lifecycle design if the measurements warrant it.

## Phase 8: One way to point into a PDF, then libraries (owner, 2026-11-14; backlog, with its core started early)

The owner's words are in `docs/OWNER-RULES.md` ("Product direction from the owner: libraries, and one way to point into a PDF"). The goal: an agent searches a folder of PDFs headless through `recto`, gets back short references, and asks for more of what a reference points at, a step at a time: the hit, its paragraph, its section, its page, its chapter, as text, markdown, a PDF made of those pages, a PNG or an SVG. Nothing here changes a PDF unless a step says so, and every step keeps rules 1, 5 and 6 and the access levels. Sizes are the coordinator's estimates until a design says otherwise.

**The idea in one paragraph.** A *locator* is one value that says where something is in a PDF, at any grain: the document (by what it is, not where it lies), a page (by number, with the page matcher's identity so it survives the file changing), a block on the page (a paragraph, heading, table or figure, by its order in reading and a short digest of its words), a stretch of characters, and the rectangles it shows at, in the one page frame `recto select` already uses. It prints two ways: a short token an agent keeps and hands back (the "condensed bit result"), and a link other programs understand, built from the standard PDF open parameters (`#page=`, `#nameddest=`, `#page=..&zoom=..,left,top`), with our own part in a key other viewers ignore. Everything that points into a page today becomes a use of it: a search hit, a contents entry's place, a highlight's lines, a link's target, a jump.

- [ ] **8.0 Remaining locator design reconciliation.** Research is delivered in `docs/research/2026-11-14-locator.md`. The page identity `recto-page/1` is implemented and shared with the matcher; the owner chose locator first with safe-removal work alongside, and our own `.recto` file shaped by KGLite with a `.kgl` export (2026-11-15). Match the report's remaining choices to those decisions before bringing only unresolved behavior to the owner. The original blanket "19 decisions wait" is obsolete; do not restart completed research.
  - What other programs understand: the PDF open parameters as Acrobat documents them and as RFC 8118 registers them for `application/pdf` (page, nameddest, zoom, view, viewrect, search and the rest), which of them PDF.js, Preview, Chrome, Edge and Skim honour, and what each does with a key it doesn't know. The W3C Web Annotation selectors (a quote with what comes before and after, a position, a fragment) as the model for a reference that finds its text again after the file changes.
  - What in a PDF stays put: the trailer `/ID`, object numbers, named destinations, the structure tree of a tagged PDF, page labels; what a re-save, a linearise, an OCR pass and an "optimise" each do to them, measured on the corpus.
  - What we already have, to be made one: the page frame (`recto_core::nav::Frame`), `select --chars`, a contents entry's `top` in thousandths, a highlight's lines and a file highlight's place in `/Annots`, `goto --within` and `--entry`, an edit's page as (source, page), the page matcher's identity (below). The design says which become the locator and which convert to it at their edge; no second coordinate system is added.
  - distillPDF's structure on real files: paragraphs, headings, tables, reading order on the corpus and on a sample of the owner's own folders (multi-column, tables, scans): how often a paragraph is right, and what it costs in time and memory per page in a helper. This decides how much "paragraph-aware" can promise.
  - The token: its grammar, its length for a hit (a target: under about 40 characters), what it must carry to be resolved with no index at hand and what it may look up, its version, and how a token made before a file changed is found again or said to be gone, never silently pointing elsewhere.
  - The `.recto` file, as a sketch for 8.5 (owner, 2026-11-14: one file at the library's root, beside the PDFs, the index itself and not a copy; "highly optimized for performance"): see 8.5 for what its design must answer; here, the two or three shapes it could take and what each costs, and that a `.recto` found in a folder is hostile input like a PDF.
  - First findings (2026-11-14, on the 141 corpus files; the report has the tables): every file has a trailer `/ID`, but Preview's re-save replaces it and two different papers share one, so it is not an identity; page object numbers survive almost no re-save; a digest of a page's decoded content (with its images) stayed the same under every re-save tried, and 0.8% of pages share one with another page of their file. So a document and a page are told by content. The token recommended so far is readable and needs no quoting, 37 characters for a search hit; its form is the owner's to choose from the report.
- [ ] **8.1 The locator in the core** (after the release: owner, 2026-11-14, "Lets do this implementation of coordinates after the release". The page matcher is still built before the release, for unsaved edits; its per-page identity is designed in 8.0 to be the locator's, so 8.1 adds the locator on top of it and builds no second one). `recto-core`: the types, the token's encode and decode (fuzzed, capped, versioned), the link form, conversion to and from the page frame and thousandths, and resolving a locator against an open document: found, moved (with where), or gone. No command and no window change. Guards: sizes, a round trip over the corpus, and old tokens after a re-save. 3–4 d
  - [x] First slice (2026-11-18): reviewed pure types/codec/geometry, compiler size guards and hostile-input fuzz target; `design/locator-core.md`. Remaining 8.1: open-document resolver, link form, changed-file/corpus round trips.
- [ ] **8.2 Search answers with references** (after 8.1, after the release). Each `recto search` hit gains `ref` (the token) and `link` (the open-parameters form); `goto`, `text`, `select`, `copy`, `render`, `snapshot`, `export svg` and `highlight` take `--at <ref>`; `recto resolve <ref>` says where it points now. One document, an open tab. The policy walks run each with a reference, and a reference to a protected document tells an agent nothing its level couldn't read. 3–4 d
- [ ] **8.3 Blocks, and asking for more** (after 8.2). A page's blocks from distillPDF in a helper process, kept in the tab's search index: paragraph, heading, list, table, figure, with reading order and the headings above. `recto get <ref> --grain hit|paragraph|section|page|chapter --as text|markdown|pdf|png|svg`, with `--next`, `--prev` and `--up`, each answer carrying the references to go further: progressive disclosure. A PDF answer is a new file made by the one writer; text stays under `untrusted`. Sections and chapters come from the file's contents or the generated ones. 6–8 d
- [ ] **8.4 The window uses it** (small, any time after 8.2): Copy Link to Selection and to a contents entry (the link form), opening a link with a reference lands where it says, and highlights and Find hits are held as locators. 2–3 d
- [x] **8.4a kglite: requirements sent, replies received, file choice decided** (owner, 2026-11-14: "Part of the plan can be improvements kglite needs to make before we implement, should we ask kglite to support a flexible format for our usecase?"). A requirements note for the kglite project, `docs/research/2026-11-14-kglite-asks.md`, readable alone. It holds seven asks (K1 a format-only crate, K2 open by header and section table, K3 partial updates by appended segments, K4 nothing on the heap while open, K5 a mapped positional text index or opaque application sections, K6 a reader safe on a hostile file, with a crafted-file panic reported, K7 a written version policy), each with what kglite does today and a test. The smallest set that lets a `.recto` file simply be a kglite file is K3, K5b and K7; using kglite's own code in the app needs all seven. The replies below and the owner's 2026-11-15 decision settle the choice: our own file, shaped by KGLite, with a `.kgl` export. Implementation and benchmarking belong to 8.5, not a new dependency decision.
  - **KGLite 0.21.6 is released, 2026-11-15** (`docs/research/2026-11-15-kglite-0.21.6-released.md`, written by the KGLite side; its figures are its own, measured on its graphs, not on our corpus): the reader hardening and the written container policy are out, and full-text search scans a string property's mapped column in place inside the one file (`text_match`, `text_hits` with byte and character offsets, `db.text.search` and `db.text.rank` with a scope chosen by the graph); no stemming, positional index or side file. Unchanged: no format crate, no partial updates, application sections not started. It doesn't change the owner's decision of 2026-11-15 (our own file, shaped by KGLite's design, with a `.kgl` export), since every save still rewrites the file. They ask back, for when 8.5 is designed: whether our rectangles and page numbers sit on nodes or on edges (on nodes, by the locator design), which query form fits the locator (inline or `within`), and the scope sizes we see (paragraphs per PDF and per section).
  - **KGLite's reply, 2026-11-14** (`docs/research/2026-11-14-kglite-reply.md`, written by the KGLite side; its claims marked measured or read there). The reader hardening (K6) and a written format policy (K7) are on KGLite's `main`, not released. Our bug note had the wrong cause: the panic came from an edge-type ordinal out of range, not from endpoint indices (those made a later save drop edges silently); three more panics were found and fixed, and a default open now never panics, with `verify="eager"` and `max_load_mb` for a file one didn't build. Full text (K5a) is taken and designed, not built: a scan in place over the mapped string column, inside the one file, with hit offsets, scoped to a node set chosen by the graph; no positional index, stemming or side file in the first version (their prototype: 1.33 GB of text, warm p95 120 ms on one thread, cold about 2 s). Application sections (K5b): later, not started, and not to be built on as it is today (a re-save drops them). **Declined:** a separate format crate (K1) and updates by appended segments (K3): every save rewrites the file, and incremental publish exists only in disk mode, as a directory. K2: one slice done (a mapped open at 1M nodes 68.5 to 22.5 ms). Their caution: the file is a KGLite file only when KGLite's code writes it; link `kglite`, or keep our own file with a `.kgl` export. **What this means for 8.5, for the owner's decision after the release:** "the smallest set" we asked for (K3, K5b, K7) is not on offer, so `.recto` as a KGLite file means linking KGLite (123 packages, rayon and zstd unconditional) and rewriting the whole file on every change to one document, which the owner's "highly optimized for performance" and a 10,000-document library speak against; the fallback named in our note stands: our own file, shaped by KGLite's design, with a `.kgl` export. They ask one thing back: whether our rectangles and page numbers sit on edges or on nodes (a graph with more than 262,144 property-carrying relationships gets no mapped topology): on nodes, by the locator design; to be answered when 8.5 is designed.
  - Decided by the owner, 2026-11-15: the fallback, our own file shaped by KGLite's design with a `.kgl` export, built with 8.5.
- [ ] **8.5 Libraries** (after 8.3; its own design report first). A library is a folder the person has granted, by name, at a real terminal or in Settings; `recto library add|remove|status`. Its index is **one `.recto` file at the library's root, beside the PDFs** (owner, 2026-11-14): it travels with the folder, is found again when the folder is moved or opened on another machine, and is never aged out by the app, since it isn't the app's to keep. Documents are indexed without tabs: one helper at a time at background priority, nothing kept per idle document. 8–12 d
  - **The `.recto` file's speed is a requirement** (owner: "this .recto file must be highly optimized for performance"). The design sets numbers for each of these and the build is measured against them, on a library of 100, 1,000 and 10,000 PDFs: time and bytes read to open it and answer the first search (the aim: map the file, read a header and the few pages a query touches, with nothing parsed or allocated per document); search time by words and by structure; the file's size against the text it indexes; time and bytes written to bring one changed PDF up to date (the aim: that document's part alone, appended, never the whole file rewritten; compacted when the dead part passes a set share); memory held while idle (none: the OS cache holds what was read) and at the peak of a query; writes per day for SSD wear.
  - **The file's kind, decided by the owner on 2026-11-15:** our own file, shaped by KGLite's design, with a `.kgl` export, built in this step (8.4a has why); of the three options in the analysis below, the third is chosen.
  - **Start from kglite** (owner, 2026-11-14: "we should lean on my kglite library. We could even use the kglite graph structure (mmapped graph) structure if it makes sense. This needs analysis. If it's over complicated for our use case we can simply be influenced by kglite as much as we want"). The analysis, in 8.0's report and settled in this step's design: what kglite's mapped graph is on disk (layout, how it is opened, what is read for a query, how it is updated and by whom), its measured speed and its known limits (the owner's own notes and benchmarks beside it), what it would bring into RectoPDF (code size, dependencies, licence, build and binary size, `unsafe`, whether it has been fuzzed against a hostile file, which a `.recto` from someone else's folder is), and how each of this step's numbers and rules comes out under three options: kglite used as it is; its graph format used with our own word index beside it; our own file, shaped by it.
  - **Its shape, for the design to settle:** a binary file read in place (fixed-layout sections, offsets not pointers, every length and offset checked before use, so it can be mapped and searched without being loaded); a table of documents by content identity with each one's path relative to the root, size and time; per document its pages' identities, blocks, headings and text positions (the locator's parts, so a reference resolves from the file alone); the word index; the graph's edges (contains, next, links to, cites) as the owner's other graph servers keep theirs, here as tables; a version, a checksum per section, and a clean way to say "written by a newer RectoPDF". "Obsidian vault style" is the graph a person and an agent can walk; whether a readable export (`recto library export`) stands beside the binary file is a decision for the owner.
  - **Hostile and shared:** a `.recto` in a folder someone sent is hostile input like a PDF: no panic, caps on every count and length, fuzzed, and nothing in it is trusted to name a file outside the root or to say what a PDF contains without the page's identity matching. Two machines or two copies of RectoPDF on a synced folder: one writer at a time by a lock that survives a crash, whole-file replacement only by atomic rename, and a reader never sees a half-written part. A read-only or full volume: the library still searches from what is there, says it is stale, and writes nowhere else without being asked.
  - **From 8.0's report, added to this step's rules:** an appended file keeps a PDF's old text until it is compacted, so a document that lost text (a redaction, a removed page) forces a compaction of its part before the library says it is up to date; the index is derived from the PDFs, so a conflict between two machines costs re-indexing and never a wrong answer; and the other items of the report's §7.4.
  - **Decisions it brings to the owner:** what a library does with a password-protected or a copy-restricted file (left out; or listed with no text); whether the file's name is `.recto` exactly, hidden, and what the Finder and sync services do with it; a readable export; and whether an agent's grant to a library covers folders inside it that have their own `.recto`.
- [ ] **8.6 Watching a library** (with or after 8.5). One file-system watch per library, not per folder or file; a changed file is re-indexed alone, found again by the page matcher's identity so references into its unchanged pages still hold; a catch-up scan when nothing was running; paused on battery and under memory pressure; `recto serve` as the headless keeper. 3–5 d
- [ ] **8.7 Search across a library** (after 8.5). `recto library search <words> [--in <library>]`, ranked across documents by words and structure (a heading counts for more than a footnote), each hit a reference; the graph's edges for "what else is near this". Meaning-based search is a later, optional layer and stays local if it comes. 5–7 d
- [ ] **8.8 Names inside the PDF** (later, on the owner's word): writing a named destination at a heading or a paragraph through the one writer, so `file.pdf#nameddest=Name` works in any reader; never on a signed file's existing bytes, and never without being asked. 2–3 d
- [ ] **8.9 A graph server for agents** (later): the library served as the owner's other graph servers are, on top of 8.7.

## Later, after the first release

Real digital signatures to the official standards, signature trust checks, Windows, Link and Join text, document-wide reflow, a second agent CLI.

## Decisions made by the owner, 2026-11-07

"I agree, but license mit is fine", to these recommendations:
- **Licence: MIT.** Third-party notices remain a Phase 7 item.
- **lopdf fork:** tag the commit a published distillPDF depends on, and move the fork's `main` to the new code once distillPDF has published.
- **Quit doesn't ask about unsaved edits,** which come back on the next launch. Closing a document still asks.
- **Agents and copy-restricted PDFs:** stays as it is, and each use of `--anyway` goes in the activity log.
- **Hidden text past today's detection:** tightened in the planned hidden-text work, without holding up selection.
- **Copies:** notice with Copy anyway; hidden text left out, with a notice.
- **SVG:** no WebP flag for now.
- **Updates:** *Check for Updates…* in the RectoPDF menu, automatic updates on, the daily check always runs. Signing secrets are added when the updater needs them.
- **State:** dormant after 5 minutes; history kept 30 days within 1 GiB, out of backups; redaction and hidden-data removal clear it.
- **Agents:** below Full an agent throws away only agents' edits; the skill keeps auto-approving read-only commands; a same-user process faking a terminal is accepted until Settings exists.
- **Exports:** peaks of 465–494 MiB at 3× and 4× are accepted.
- **A heavy tab sleeps when you leave it.** Off-screen workers stay within the 256 MiB budget, so a map whose worker alone is over it goes dormant on leaving, and coming back costs its redraw of about 750 ms over an instant preview. Memory comes first. (Owner: "I agree".)
- **Undoing a delete past a Save** keeps only the removed pages, within the 64 MiB per PDF, so it works whatever the size of the PDF. Built with Save, on the streaming rewrite. Until then a PDF over 64 MiB can undo turns and moves past a Save, not a delete. Clones aren't allowed past 64 MiB. (Owner: "I agree with you".)
- **Tab names:** the file name, with the full name and title on hover.
- **Before a public release:** a name search for the trademark, no registration yet; wait for Anthropic's written answer and add an API-key route.

## Decisions made by the owner, 2026-11-08

- **Structure** (after the architecture review): "let's do the quick fixes now before release, then add the large restructuring into the plan before we start on the other features". Steps 1–3 in phase 7; steps 4–13 as phase R, after the release and before phases 4–6's other features.

"I agree with all your recommendations":
- **The lopdf streaming rewrite** goes to the fork's `work/streaming-rewrite` branch.
- **Dark paper:** the text-weight curve applies to text and lines; coloured areas in flipped images keep the gentler old curve, so charts keep richer colours.
- **Usage rights (UR3), as on IRS forms:** a rewrite such as a page delete allows a normal Save, removes the usage rights and shows a quiet note. Usage rights aren't a person's signature. Signed files still follow decision 27.
- **`recto text` marks headers, footers and page numbers** in a separate field next to hidden spans; the text itself is unchanged.
- **Copying images and snapshots from copy-restricted PDFs** gets the same notice with Copy anyway as text.
- **The first Save keeps one backup of the original.**
- **Later:** signing secrets in a protected `release` environment with the owner as required reviewer; a public release waits for Anthropic's written answer and adds an API-key route; a trademark name search before release.

- **Defaults taken, 2026-11-08** (from the decisions waiting, both "yes" by default, as the coordinator asked): paper colour moved from the `⋯` panel to View > Paper, the panel keeping to tools; the sidebar footer's "N of M" removed, as the page number is always in the lower right.

## Decisions waiting on you

The current open decisions are under "Decisions still requiring the owner" near the top. The entries immediately above record earlier decisions; they do not reopen them.

- **Phase R, 2026-11-12:** go-ahead given ("Yes you have the goahead"). Taken as the coordinator's recommendations, not the owner's own words, and open to change: a local full check may run only the tests a change can affect, with CI still running everything on every branch before it merges (R0.2); the pilot feature for R4.3 is highlight annotations.
